# Research Intake Tracker

The single place where every recommendation from Redlamp's research gets a decision, a phase and a status. It covers these studies:

- [AI and computational photography findings](ai-findings.md) (source tag **AI**)
- [darktable study](darktable-findings.md) (source tag **DT**)
- [Topaz-style upscaling and sharpening supplement](notes/H-topaz-upscale-sharpen.md) (source tag **H**)
- [Library and catalog findings](library-findings.md) (source tag **LIB**)
- [Tethered capture findings](tethering-findings.md) (source tag **TC**)
- [RapidRAW study](rapidraw-findings.md) (source tag **RR**)
- [RAWmakase study](rawmakase-findings.md) (source tag **RMK**)
- [RawRefinery study](rawrefinery-findings.md) (source tag **RRF**)

Where studies recommend the same thing, the row is merged and cites each. *Last updated: 9 October 2026.*

## How to use this tracker

- **Every row starts as `Proposed`.** A row moves to `Accepted`, `Rejected` or `Deferred` only when the project owner decides, and the decision date goes in the Notes column. Items in the "Recommended" column are the research's suggestion, not a commitment.
- **Status** is tracked separately from the decision: `Not started`, `In progress`, `Blocked` (name the blocking row), `Done` (name the commit).
- **Change the tracker in the same pull request as the work.** A row is `Done` when the code, tests and docs land.
- **Review cadence:** walk the `Proposed` rows at each phase planning, and the `Blocked` rows weekly.
- **Adding rows:** new research or new ideas get a new ID in the right section; never reuse an ID.
- **GitHub issues mirror the work items:** each open row has an issue, kept in step by `scripts/tracker-issues.py` (decisions and skips stay here only). Plan here, then sync; issues people file come in as new rows. See `.cursor/rules/tracker-issues.mdc`.

**Columns**

| Column | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| ID | Stable identifier, prefixed by area |
| Item | What to do |
| Recommended | The research verdict: **Adopt** (take the idea), **Do better** (same capability, our own design), **Build** (no usable prior art), **Skip** |
| Phase | Recommended roadmap phase (P1 is now; P5 is iPad and iPhone, after 1.0) |
| Size | **S** ≤ 1 engineer-week, **M** 1–3, **L** 3–6, **XL** > 6. Figures in engineer-weeks (ew) come from the AI findings; sizes without figures are estimates made for this tracker |
| Depends on | Rows that must be done or decided first |
| Decision | `Proposed` / `Accepted` / `Rejected` / `Deferred` |
| Status | `Not started` / `In progress` / `Blocked` / `Done` |
| Source | Where the evidence is |

---

| DEC-54 | How does the library land on main, which takes only a straight line? | A short series of commits by part, the branch kept as `library/catalog-history` | LIB rows | Accepted (2026-10-10), otherwise: every library commit rebased onto main, conflicts resolved again commit by commit, and every hash the docs cite rewritten by subject | The library board (2026-10-10) |

| DEC-56 | Rename Photos and Move Edits are sheets, which macOS 26 holds about 0.3 s as they open and as they close: keep them, or windows of their own, or child windows? | Keep them as sheets | LIB-26, LIB-11 | Accepted (2026-10-10): kept as sheets | The library board (2026-10-10) |
| DEC-57 | While an arrow key repeats, may the right panel show the selection at most every 100 ms, for held arrows within 8.3 ms at 20,000 photos? | Yes | LIB-14 | Declined (2026-10-10): the panels follow every step, about 9 ms a press while a key is held | The library board (2026-10-10) |
| DEC-58 | Do names sort as the Finder does (`DSC_5513` before `DSC05507`) or in Folders' order (`DSC05507` first)? | The Finder's | LIB-06, LIB-10 | Accepted (2026-10-10): the Finder's order, for the library and Folders alike | The library board (2026-10-10) |
| DEC-59 | A photo whose file is deleted or moved outside Redlamp: dropped as today, kept as missing in its folder with a badge as Lightroom does, or kept as missing only in Library Health's check? | Only in Library Health's check, with Locate… and Remove, out of other lists | LIB-40 | Accepted (2026-10-10): kept as missing only in Library Health's check, with Locate… and Remove | The library board (2026-10-10) |

## 3. Cameras and raw data

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CAM-02 | Measured per-camera white levels, plus clip detection that needs a spike of pixels at the maximum (replaces LibRaw's per-image heuristic) | Do better | P2 | M | CAM-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | In progress (1bb4b65): clip-spike detection replaces LibRaw's per-image heuristic (the Fuji sample's white had fallen to one stray photosite); the measured per-camera table waits on clipped sample shots per body (DN-05) | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-03 | Apply DNG GainMap (OpcodeList2) by default in normalization | Adopt | P2 | S | — | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (7f2827b): OpcodeList2 GainMaps applied on the GPU after hot-pixel repair and highlight reconstruction, before demosaicing (linear DNGs per channel); Pixel 4a fixture added, corners 2.9x brighter. Noise reduction and the dual-demosaic blend scale the noise model by the gain per channel (a 64-texel field over the frame), so shaded corners are denoised as strongly as the centre: behind a 3x gain, 77% of flat noise used to survive where 54% did elsewhere; now the two are within 15% | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-04 | Dual-illuminant color: interpolated DNG matrices plus ForwardMatrix | Do better | P2 | M | P1-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done: DNGs read ColorMatrix, CameraCalibration, ForwardMatrix, CalibrationIlluminant 1 and 2 and AnalogBalance, and render with them interpolated in inverse colour temperature (the as-shot temperature found by iterating the interpolation, otherwise the Temperature slider). ForwardMatrix is used when present, as the DNG specification prefers; without it, daylight matches LibRaw's matrix exactly. The Pixel 4a's ForwardMatrix is gentler than its ColorMatrix, so its saturated colours render noticeably softer than before. The Temperature and Tint model still uses one matrix | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-05 | Paper-based Bayer demosaics (LMMSE, Menon 2007 or adaptive residual interpolation) instead of RCD and AMaZE | Do better | P2 | L | P1-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (17aa648): Menon, Andriani & Calvagno 2007 from the paper, four GPU passes; beats Malvar by 0.15–0.6 dB colour PSNR on binned sample crops and makes 65% less zone-plate false colour. The paper's optional refinement step not implemented | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-06 | Dual demosaic: blend a sharp and a smooth result by local contrast, with an automatic threshold | Adopt | P2 | S | CAM-05 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (e89acb2): Menon's green gives way to the plain four-neighbour average where the neighbours span under 3 noise sigmas (fully by 6), threshold from the noise model; flat-noise green error down 19%, detail unchanged | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-07 | Markesteijn X-Trans demosaic from LibRaw's CDDL source | Adopt | P2 | M | DEC-40 | Accepted (2026-10-07): the owner chose to port it under CDDL (DEC-40) and to change rendering for every X-Trans edit rather than gate it by process version, since it fixes a known gap in a pre-alpha | Done (02eaefb4): one pass on the GPU in bands of 256 rows (three passes measured no better), in two CDDL-1.0 files; it matches LibRaw's own output to 41–87 dB on the DN-11 test set. On noise-free X-Trans mosaics a sharp edge's MTF50 rises from 0.185 to 0.345 cycles per pixel (the lens gives 0.347), zone-plate false colour falls from 0.084 to 0.012, and photos gain 4.4 dB; with Luminance 50 it gains 1.8 dB at ISO 3200-like noise and 0.7 dB at 12800, equal at 51200, keeping 1.4 to 1.7 times the texture, and real RawNIND X-Trans pairs score the same binned. NoiseCalibration's X-Trans tables were re-measured (the test helper gave X-Trans 4 black levels for 36 positions; fixed), and the X-T3's process references re-recorded at every version: existing X-Trans edits render sharper, with more of their grain at low Luminance | [DT §9](darktable-findings.md#9-licensing-of-reusable-pieces), [DN-11 §5.2](notes/DN-11-lightroom-raw-denoise.md#52-demosaicing-alone-the-raw-details-question): the generic interpolation keeps half a clean edge's resolution, with ten times a learned demosaic's false colour |
| CAM-08 | CFA-domain highlight reconstruction (estimate clipped channels from unclipped neighbors) | Adopt | P2 | M | P1-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (6789502): no clip at 1 after white balance; clipped photosites rebuilt from bright unclipped neighbours in cube-root space with a colour offset from the bright rim (constant-chromaticity Zhang & Brainard 2004); fully blown areas become the lowest consistent neutral. One-photosite detail against a rebuilt sky shows more demosaic false colour (CAM-05, CAM-06) | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-09 | Segmentation-based reconstruction of fully blown highlights | Adopt | P3 | M | CAM-08 | Proposed | Not started | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-10 | JPEG XL DNG reading via libjxl (moved from P4) | Adopt | P2 | M | — | Proposed | In progress (57ba38e): linear JPEG XL DNGs (iPhone ProRAW) decode through ImageIO rather than libjxl, mapped through the LinearizationTable; JPEG XL mosaic DNGs are refused with an error for now | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-11 | Keep LibRaw for decoding; don't adopt rawspeed | Skip rawspeed | — | — | — | Proposed | — | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-12 | Nikon's High Efficiency NEFs (HE and HE*, from the Z 8, Z 9, Z 6III, Z f, Z5 II and Z50 II), which LibRaw can't read: LibRaw#826's community decoder, fixed and held to a bench of CC0 samples, carried in Redlamp's LibRaw fork (CAM-30) until LibRaw's own decoder ships | Adopt | P2 | M | CAM-30, DEC-54 | Accepted (2026-10-10): the owner chose to carry LibRaw#826's decoder, fixed in place, in Redlamp's LibRaw fork rather than wait for LibRaw's snapshot; DEC-54 cleared it for a release on 2026-10-10 | Done (f94fc8f; the fork at 4abfcd2): HE and HE* files from the Z 9, Z 8, Z f, Z 6III, Z5 II and Z50 II open, other bodies' (the ZR's) are refused until a sample passes the fork's bench. [LibRaw#826](https://github.com/LibRaw/LibRaw/pull/826)'s decoder with RdWing's four fixes decoded the first five bodies' 22 files correctly; the fork fixed the rest: a layout per decode (two sizes decoding at once crashed it), band sizes and significance lengths as JPEG XS defines them (the fitted ones crashed the Z50 II's 3:2 and 16:9 files and garbled its 1:1 ones), failed precincts and malformed streams refused (20 of 32 fuzzing runs had crashed it). The bench holds all 28 HE and HE* files to recorded mosaics; against their lossless shots their colour balance agrees within 0.11% (the Z50 II) to 3.3% (the Z 6III, whose leaves move), and they pass the sanitizers, parallel decodes and 16,000 mutated files. The decode and colour suites have HE and HE* samples of all six bodies; the Z5 II's and Z50 II's colour references record them without a colour matrix, which waits on CAM-21. On a Z 6III HE* file posted with Adobe DNG Converter's DNG of it, the decode matches Adobe's in every photosite (fork b9fd8fc). Decoding takes about twice LibRaw's lossless time ([CAM-12 note](notes/CAM-12-nikon-high-efficiency.md#testing-libraw826-9-october-2026)) | [Camera coverage set](../../tests/decode/samples.json), [CAM-12 note](notes/CAM-12-nikon-high-efficiency.md) |
| CAM-13 | Move to a LibRaw release that lists the bodies 0.22.2 doesn't: the Sony A7 V is refused, and the A1 II decodes with a black strip along the bottom | Adopt | P2 | S | — | Accepted (2026-10-09): the owner chose LibRaw master's head over waiting for a public snapshot | Done (d12a989): LibRaw is pinned at master `7bfffe2` (9 October 2026), 106 commits past 0.22.2 with every fix of 0.22.2's stable branch (each has a same-subject commit on master; the rest are its version and changelog commits). The Sony A7 V opens through Sony's ARW6 decoder and passes the camera bench on a raw.pixls.us sample; the A1 II's YCbCr sample opens at 4332 × 2892 without the strip 0.22.2 left (which also turned and framed it wrongly), but with the magenta cast every Sony YCbCr ARW has (CAM-21). The 27 decode regression samples decode exactly as before, so no other body moved. Diagnostics and bench reports name the commit, "LibRaw 0.22.0-Release (7bfffe2)", since master still reports 0.22.0. Nikon's High Efficiency decoder isn't in master yet ([LibRaw#826](https://github.com/LibRaw/LibRaw/pull/826)), so CAM-12 waits on its own | [Camera coverage set](../../tests/decode/samples.json), [RMK §4.1](rawmakase-findings.md#41-raws-libraw-and-cameras) |
| CAM-14 | Camera bench: decode diagnostics (LibRaw's decoder, bits, frame and firmware; black level against optical black, the clip spike, dark edge strips) and a comparison of the default rendering with the camera's embedded JPEG (orientation, framing, structure, exposure, neutral cast, highlights), as versioned checks in a report format, with `redlamp camera-bench`; calibrated on the camera coverage set | Build | P2 | M | CAM-01 | Accepted (2026-10-04): the owner approved the camera bench plan | Done (9a56213, d318352, a83e03e, 6ed9782, 91c635d, 47633b6): each raw decode carries its identity (LibRaw's camera, decoder, bits, frame, firmware, previews) and measurements (black against the masked margins and the 0.1th percentile, the clip point, dark edge strips, the colour matrix); `CameraBench` checks them and compares the default rendering with the camera's embedded JPEG (orientation, framing, structure, exposure, neutral cast, highlights, colour difference), as versioned checks in a closed report format (`docs/camera-bench.schema.json`), with `redlamp camera-bench`. No check fails on the 26 samples of the 25 verified cameras, and five faults put into a Sony and a Canon decode are each caught by their own check. The run over raw.pixls.us (`scripts/camera-bench-seed.py`, 820 photos in 742 camera modes) found monochrome raws refused, bodies newer than LibRaw 0.22.2 opening wrongly, SuperCCD files crashing the decoder and more (CAM-20 to CAM-26, [note](notes/CAM-14-seed-run.md)); its false alarms set decode.black 3 and decode.edges 2 | The owner (2026-10-04) |
| CAM-15 | The Camera Bench window: photos or a folder, grouped by camera mode, a few photos per mode chosen to cover what the evidence still needs, results beside side-by-side pairs with the camera's JPEG, one question, and the exact measurements before sending | Build | P2 | M | CAM-14, UX-10 | Accepted (2026-10-04): the owner approved the camera bench plan | Done (5361ef1, 623b61c, 6626305): Help › Test Your Camera… and the command palette open the Camera Bench window: photos or a folder, grouped by camera mode, up to eight per mode for what each still needs, tested in an engine of its own through the decode service; each mode's checks, side-by-side pairs, what it still needs and one question; What's Sent shows the exact JSON; Report This Problem opens Report a Bug or Send Feedback under Photos & Cameras | The owner (2026-10-04) |

It applies only under that look, so no edit renders differently and no process version is needed; anchoring every render is CAM-29, and a shipped per-camera table, measured by Redlamp and never taken from Adobe's data, can follow | Build | P2 | M | CAM-14, UX-32 | Accepted (2026-10-09): the owner accepted it from a museum imaging department's feedback, to land before 0.3.0 alongside the library; design approved (2026-10-09): anchoring only under Redlamp Reproduction, each camera calibrated from the user's target with a typical anchor until then, and the stops readout only under that look, counting Exposure ([design](../plans/2026-10-09-reproduction-design.md)) | Done (commits "Exposure anchored to the camera under Redlamp Reproduction", "The histogram's readout in stops under Redlamp Reproduction", "Calibrate from Target", [design](../plans/2026-10-09-reproduction-design.md)): under Redlamp Reproduction each edit carries its photo's camera's exposure anchor (`exposureAnchor` in the sidecar), the camera's calibration or the typical +1.03 EV, marked not calibrated; it's written when the look is chosen, pasted, synced or applied with a recipe, never copied between photos and never kept in a `.redrecipe`, and it takes BaselineExposure's place for the render and Auto. Calibrate from Target (under the Base Look, in the Photo menu and ⌘K): a click on a white-balanced grey patch and its reference L*, then Calibrate [camera], kept per camera in Application Support, or Set This Photo's Exposure; Update Calibration brings a later calibration into an edit. The histogram's line adds the exposure in stops from the anchored 18% grey, with ≈ while it's typical. The CLI takes `--anchor`. Whites and Blacks stay endpoints (measured). Not done: a comparison with Lightroom's exports, and edge-aware Clarity (TON-06); shipping waits on DEC-05 | [DT §4](darktable-findings.md#4-color-science-and-tone) |
| TON-06 | Clarity, Texture and Sharpening on one multiscale decomposition, with deconvolution-style capture sharpening | Do better | P2 | L | ARC-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done: sharpening renders behind Lightroom's four controls, now noise-aware with deconvolution by Detail (SHP-01), and global Texture and Clarity render as gains on log-luminance bands between pyramid levels (about 1 ms). Edge-aware Clarity done (process 9, [design](../plans/2026-10-03-edge-aware-clarity-design.md)): the band ends at a self-guided filter's base at Clarity's scale instead of pyramid level 6, so at Clarity +100 on 13 sky photos the land beside the skyline darkens by 0.06 EV against 0.13, with the texture's boost kept (1.36), and the land's overall tone no longer moves (+0.02 EV against −0.08); 38 ms per photo for the map. One decomposition done (641d8ee, process 11, [design](../plans/2026-10-03-detail-decomposition-design.md)): four undecimated à-trous scales of the noise-reduced image's luminance per work area, read by Texture (a 0.25-stop soft limit, gain 1.6), Clarity (against its edge-preserving base) and sharpening's noise separator (Richardson–Lucy kept: as band gains it loses 0.1–0.8 dB). Texture's change beside strong edges drops from 1.0–1.7 stops to about 0.3 with the same boost on texture; Texture and Clarity drags 2.6–5.5 ms to 0.2–0.3 ms at 1:1 and fit level 1, 19–21 ms to 11–13 ms over a whole 24 MP frame; Luminance drags slower (5.8 to 9.7 ms at 1:1); the preview matches exports (ARC-04) except Texture's correlation at 700 px (about 0.8). Slice 1 done: `DNGProfile` reads profile tags from DNGs and `.dcp` files, and process 4 applies the HueSatMap after the camera matrix, in linear ProPhoto, both illuminants' maps blended by colour temperature as the matrices are. It matches a CPU port of the DNG SDK's `RefBaselineHueSatMap` to half-float precision (2.5D and 3D sRGB-encoded maps); unlike the SDK, light above white keeps its headroom. The Pixel moves by mean ΔE2000 3.0 (worst patch 6.1), the expected Adobe Standard correction; its camera golden is re-recorded and process-4 recipe goldens recorded. Slice 2 done: a profile's LookTable and tone curve are baked into a 33³ scene-referred Base Look (dng_sdk's spline and hue-preserving RGB tone method; Redlamp's tone curve when the profile has none) and offered under "In This Photo" in the Base Look menu; new edits keep Redlamp Color, and choosing it keeps the baked table with the installed looks. Embedded looks never travel in exported recipes. Finding: Apple ProRAW's tone curve (0.18 to 0.12) follows its ProfileGainTableMap, a local tone map, and alone renders far too dark. The map now applies (process 5): ProfileGainTableMap and ProfileGainTableMap2 are read (big-endian, 6 × 8 and 12 × 16 grids of 257-point tables on the samples, gains 0.07–4.3) and applied in the develop kernel in linear ProPhoto after BaselineExposure, as one trilinear sample of a 3D texture, matching the specification's arithmetic to 0.004. Rendered under Redlamp Color it looked washed out (shadows lifted twice), so it comes only with the photo's embedded Apple look, as strongly as the look's Amount, which renders ProRAW the way the iPhone does; Redlamp Color renders ProRAW as before. `.dcp` import (slice 3) is deferred (2026-10-02): the owner doesn't use DCP files | [DT §4](darktable-findings.md#4-color-science-and-tone) |

The mask overlay and the samplers map through the same geometry, so handles sit on their content in a cropped, straightened, turned or mirrored frame, and a radial mask's outline, handles and rotation turn with the frame | [DT §5](darktable-findings.md#5-modules-filters-and-masks) |

Where the photo has no edge to follow, a trust coefficient (the guide's variance against epsilon) keeps the mask's own. On a 4096-wide test with a mask at half the analysis grid, the 20–80% ramp narrows from at least 4 px to at most 2; on the Nikon sample the +1.5 EV of a Subject mask no longer spills onto the background beside the origami star. Only edits moved to process 13 change (the gate's retouch references differ, the rest are identical). Before it, AI mask edges were already solved per pixel when masks are made (`ClosedFormMatte`, `SkyMatte`; MSK-17), Refine Edges snaps them with a guided filter, and the Refine Edge brush (an AI component's menu) solves them again where it paints, its strokes kept with the mask for Update AI Masks. Shipping waits on DEC-05, as the guided filter's other uses do. Learnt only from pixels more than 2 px from any it took as sky, and with coverage from 0.12 to 0.88 left where the colour puts it, the halo falls from 2.38 to 2.09 ΔE (better on every sky) and the rim from +2.12 to +1.11 L*, band error 0.039 to 0.034 (2a2b0968, `sky_coverage.py`); on the evaluation set mixed pixels gain 0.011 of coverage (median), and no photo gains or loses over a quarter of coverage on more than 0.6% of its frame. The coarse mask's few percent of sky kept over foreground beyond the band, which darkened dark foreground under a sky edit, now stay only as far as the colour allows (5ee859e7): halo 2.09 to 2.00 ΔE, wires-overcast 1.83 to 1.09. Left: dense pale crowns where the models see no sky (counting sky the colour sees but the pass didn't take back recovers some between twigs, but takes snow under an overcast sky for sky); sky-coloured foreground beside the edge, such as glass reflecting the sky, which colour can't tell from sky (2a2b0968 takes a little more of it, 0.24 to 0.36 on one facade); and sky that darkens towards the frame's sides (vignetting) where the coarse mask left it out, whose colour the sky estimate carries in from brighter sky (most of what the bench still misses; the true sky colour would take the halo from 2.10 to 1.99 ΔE) | The owner (2026-10-06): the sky's aura, and trees |
| MSK-29 | Subject and People edges that hold up: a person's Subject edge from their person mask, Vision's mask at the matte render's size rather than shrunk and blurred, ClosedFormMatte in linear light, a band that follows flyaway hair, less haze over smooth and dark backgrounds, missed limbs filled as missed heads are | Adopt | P2 | M | MSK-25 | Accepted (2026-10-06): the owner chose better starting masks and hair-aware matting first | In progress: two changes measured and not shipped (6dca956). A person's Subject edge from their person mask took the error around the edge from 0.062 to 0.059 on five portraits, but on the evaluation set Vision's person masks miss backlit rims, low-key hair and fur held beside people, and take in shadows, so 4 of the 8 most-changed photos got worse; SAM 3's hair keeping ViTMatte's strands near hair dropped stems beside people but backlit flyaways too. Vision's person instances also take birds for people. Next: a band that follows flyaways, missed limbs, crowds | The owner (2026-10-06): stray hairs, masks past the subject |
| MSK-30 | People's face parts (Face Skin, Eyebrows, Eye Sclera, Iris and Pupil, Lips, Teeth) measured on photos, then matted inside the face or found by SAM 3 on a crop of each face, instead of landmark polygons with a 2 px feather | Adopt | P3 | M | MSK-25 | Proposed | In progress: measured on the evaluation set's 18 faces (face_parts.py, daeb9a7). Lips follow the outer edge, loosely on some faces; Teeth covers only the middle teeth, 45% to 91% of the tooth-coloured pixels in the open mouth (67% on average) with up to a fifth on lips or gums; Eyebrows are drawn even where a fringe hides them; Vision finds no face in eye close-ups. Faces are numbered left to right (Vision returned them in no set order, so a part's number could change between runs), and found on face rectangles as well as by landmarks alone, which missed both faces of groups-01 (9fe7001e). Next: Lips and Teeth matted inside the mouth by colour (no new model); hidden brows need hair, which only SAM 3 gives | The owner (2026-10-06) |
| MSK-31 | Refine Edges sized for 4096 px masks (now a guided filter of radius 32 against a guide upsampled from 2048), and Edge and Feather that keep a mask's partial coverage | Adopt | P2 | S | MSK-18 | Accepted (2026-10-07): the owner chose process version 14 for Edge and Feather | Done (2026-10-07; 9124a28, 27912034): Refine Edges solves the edge again per pixel, as masks of its kind are made: on hair_bench's heads an error of 0.089 and 49% of strands kept from today's mattes, 0.076 and 31% from coarse masks, against the guided filter's 0.136 and 16%. From process 14, Feather and Edge shape the mask's body and add its stray hairs back (faded by an inward Edge's share): at Feather 50, 48% of strands kept against 35%, with less background taken in (`edge_feather.py`) | The owner (2026-10-06) |
| MSK-32 | ViTMatte edge refinement for Subject, Background, People and Objects: its published weights converted to Core ML and run over the unsure band at the size masks are stored at (4096 px, tiled), wherever it measures better than ClosedFormMatte; shipped under DEC-35 | Adopt | P2 | M | DEC-35, MSK-25 | Accepted (2026-10-06): the owner chose it | Done (2026-10-07): converted to Core ML for 1024 px tiles with 8-bit weights (109 MB; 519017a) and published as models-vitmatte-base-v1 (DEC-26). On its own over a band 5% wide it halves closed-form matting's edge error on hair_bench's heads but pulls background into the matte on real photos (5fe15cf); the app keeps closed-form's matte and adds only the strands ViTMatte finds beyond it (c3d3cc3): on the quality gate's strand scene 69% of strand pixels against 31%, and on the evaluation set over a quarter more coverage on at most 0.07% of a frame, mostly hair. Offered to everyone in Settings › Models. Limiting it to SAM 3's hair goes with MSK-29 | [C-masking §5.2](notes/C-masking.md#52-learned-matting-shortlist) |

## 7. Lenses and geometry

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LNS-01 | One lens-correction engine: every source baked into per-image 1-D tables (distortion per channel, vignetting, optical center, tangential terms) | Do better | P2 | L | — | Accepted (2026-10-02): the owner asked for lens profile corrections | In progress: `LensCorrection` holds every source as tables over the radius from the optical centre (red, green and blue's recorded scale, the vignetting gain). `GeometryMap` applies it after the manual Distortion slider (overlays, work areas and Constrain to Image included), zoomed just enough to keep the frame filled when a correction pulls the edges in, and the develop kernel reads it as a 64-entry table, sampling red and blue apart where the profile corrects colour fringes. A synthetic render matches the CPU geometry to 0.0008 on average. Tangential terms aren't carried (they don't fit a radial table; phones write zero) | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-02 | Embedded manufacturer corrections (Sony, Fujifilm, OM System, Panasonic, DNG opcodes), moved from P3; validated against in-camera JPEGs | Adopt | P2 | M | LNS-01 | Accepted (2026-10-02): taken instead of lensfun while DEC-04 waits on counsel | In progress: DNG OpcodeList3 WarpRectilinear and FixVignetteRadial (from the specification) and Sony's ARW tags 0x7032, 0x7035 and 0x7037 (the conventions darktable and RawTherapee use) apply by default from process 5 (Enable Profile Corrections, with Distortion and Vignetting amounts). The Sony sample moves by mean ΔE2000 3.3, and its frame edge now sits where the camera's JPEG has it (39% across a corner crop, against 40% corrected and 43% before); the Pixel's warp is the identity. Fujifilm's RAF tags (0xF00B, 0xF00F and 0xF010 in the FujiIFD, 9 or 11 knots) follow from process 6, since process 5 had already shipped: on the X-T2 sample they correct a 9% barrel at the corners, and the corrected render matches the camera's JPEG with an edge correlation of 0.93 (0.62 uncorrected; every other sign or radius convention 0.69 at best). Its fringe tables agree with Remove Chromatic Aberration's measurement of the same photo (red −0.0013 against −0.0018, blue +0.0016 against +0.0015). Fujifilm's 1.25× crop modes, OM System and Panasonic not started | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-03 | lensfun models reimplemented from their published math; database shipped as data | Adopt | P2 | M | LNS-01, DEC-04 | Proposed | Not started | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-04 | Import user-supplied Adobe LCP files with our own reader; never ship or convert Adobe's | Adopt | P2 | S | LNS-01 | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner approved it for agent wave 1 | Done (0c8729c, 45e252c, 62c8574): our own reader for the LCP XML (both RDF forms) and the Adobe Camera Model's rectilinear distortion, lateral chromatic aberration and vignetting (Chen et al., 2010), tabulated into `LensCorrection` with source `profile`; profiles in `~/Library/Application Support/Redlamp/Lens Profiles/` are matched by camera and lens (EXIF and LibRaw spellings, then the lens's display name) and interpolated in focal length and aperture. A correction the file carries wins; a crop mode's frame (shape or size off the profile's) gets none; it applies from process 5, under the profile-corrections switch, in the app and CLI only (engine tests never read a developer's profiles). Not done: fisheye profiles, tangential terms, focus-distance interpolation, the matched profile's name in the Lens panel | [DT §9](darktable-findings.md#9-licensing-of-reusable-pieces) |
| LNS-05 | All geometry (crop, Transform, orientation, lens) as one inverse map, sampling the pyramid once | Do better | P2 | L | LNS-01, MSK-01 | Accepted (2026-10-01) | In progress: `GeometryMap` composes crop with its angle, the manual Transform (a virtual camera tilted, turned and rolled, then stretched, scaled and offset), and the user's rotation and flip into one homography from the developed frame to the photo; the develop kernel applies it per pixel and samples the pyramid once, at the level its Jacobian picks, and white fills where no photo remains. Constrain to Image shrinks the crop about its centre until it fits. Manual lens Distortion is one radial polynomial after the homography (the renderer needs only the forward model; overlays invert it by Newton steps), and manual Vignetting is a gain on the recorded photo; profile and embedded corrections will feed the same step | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-06 | Crop and guides copied from Lightroom, parameters mapping to Lightroom's crop fields; post-crop vignette follows the crop | Adopt | P2 | M | LNS-05 | Accepted (2026-10-01) | Done: the Crop & Straighten tool (R) shows the whole straightened frame with the crop over it (thirds grid, corner and edge handles, drag to move), aspect presets with a lock (A), the Angle slider, Constrain to Image (which fits the crop as drawn again after every Angle or Transform change), rotate (⌘[ / ⌘]) and flip, and Reset; vignette and grain follow the crop. Overlays (Grid, Thirds, Diagonal, Golden Triangle, Golden Ratio, Golden Spiral and Aspect Ratios, in Lightroom's order; 76de46a) cycle with `O`, and `⇧O` turns the triangle and the spiral (eight ways), and `X` swaps the crop between portrait and landscape, in the Crop tool only. The Straighten tool (its button, or ⌘-drag in the crop) levels the photo along a drawn horizon or vertical. Lightroom's `crs:Crop*` fields map to Redlamp's crop (0e2aea7, `LightroomCrop`, checked against Redlamp's crop geometry for every orientation; applied once XMP sidecars import, EDT-12). Choose Overlays to Cycle and Choose Aspect Ratios (2a5b543): checkboxes in a popover beside the Overlay menu; `O` steps only through the chosen overlays, the Aspect Ratios overlay outlines only the chosen ratios, both remembered | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-07 | Our own line detector from the LSD paper | Build | P2 | M | DEC-05 | Accepted (2026-10-02): the owner asked for automatic Upright; to stay clear of DEC-05, the detector follows Burns, Hanson and Riseman's line-support regions (1986) and leaves out LSD's a-contrario validation | Done: `LineDetector` grows regions of pixels whose gradients agree within 22.5° on the 1024-pixel analysis image (EXIF-oriented, square-root luma, a light blur first) and fits each long, thin, dense region with its gradient-weighted principal axis; 50–420 ms per photo. It finds a turned rectangle's sides within 0.5° in noise and no long lines in noise alone. Stair-stepped (aliased) edges break into short runs, which real photos' soft edges don't | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-08 | Upright as a virtual camera rotation (Level, Vertical, Full, Guided all in late P2, deterministic Auto), mapping to Lightroom's Transform sliders; optional 4-corner rectangle mode | Do better | P2 | L | LNS-05, LNS-07 | Accepted (2026-10-01) | In progress: Guided Upright works. The Transform sliders are a virtual camera tilted, turned and rolled; drawing two to four guides solves the Vertical, Horizontal and Rotate that make them upright or level (a few Levenberg–Marquardt steps on their angles, damped so undetermined sliders stay put), and the guides stay on their edges in the photo's coordinates. Auto, Level, Vertical and Full now work from the detected edges (LNS-07): lines within 30° of vertical or level, a robust (Cauchy) fit over Rotate, Rotate and Vertical, or all three, and a correction only when the edges agree on it (most of their weight within 1.5° afterwards, spread over a quarter of the frame, inside the sliders' range), falling back Full to Vertical to Level to nothing. Auto halves perspective past 30 and eases it until Constrain to Image keeps 80% of the frame. A facade shot looking up (Vertical −25) is recovered within 2; on the six sample photos, none architectural, Auto levels by 0.1–1° and corrects only the product shot's verticals (5.2), where a first version without the agreement test warped all of them | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-09 | Chromatic aberration: profile and embedded TCA in the warp, automatic lateral CA, Lightroom-style Defringe | Adopt | P2 | M | LNS-01 | Accepted (2026-10-02): the owner asked for it | Done: a profile's own fringe correction (Sony's) applies with the profile; otherwise Remove Chromatic Aberration measures it, in 48 tiles at the finest pyramid level up to 3072 pixels, with a Lucas–Kanade step along the radius at edges across it and a fit of a·r + b·r³ per channel (17–57 ms per photo, once). It recovers a synthetic lens within 3·10⁻⁴ of its scale and cuts fringe misalignment by over 70%; on the Sony sample it agrees in sign and order with Sony's own tables, and on the Fujifilm one it removes visible red-blue fringes (about 6 pixels at the corners). Defringe greys purple or green (Lightroom's Amount and Hue ranges, as OKLab hue bands) within two texels of a stop or more of contrast, and leaves the same colours elsewhere | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-10 | Guided automatic defringe | Adopt | P3 | M | LNS-09 | Proposed | Not started | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-11 | The Lens Corrections panel names the matched lens profile and lists profiles that couldn't be read | Adopt | P2 | S | LNS-04 | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner chose wave 1's follow-ups for agent wave 2a | Done (753c807): `LensCorrection.profileName` (not used in rendering) names a profile correction for its lens; the panel shows "Profile: ‹lens›" under Enable Profile Corrections and, when some profiles in the folder couldn't be read, how many, with files and reasons in its help | This tracker |
| LNS-12 | Custom crop aspect ratios, as Lightroom's Enter Custom…: a ratio (21 × 9) or a size in pixels (1080 × 1350) typed in and kept in the Aspect menu as a preset; and a button beside the menu that swaps the crop between portrait and landscape, as `X` does | Adopt | P2 | S | LNS-06 | Accepted (2026-10-08): the owner accepted it from #352 | Not started: the Aspect menu offers only Custom (free), Original, 1 × 1, 4 × 5, 5 × 7, 2 × 3 and 16 × 9 (`CropAspect`), and only `X` swaps the orientation, which the Overlay menu's help mentions | [#352](https://github.com/pdcgomes/redlamp/issues/352), sent from the app (2026-10-08): ratios typed in as a ratio or a resolution and saved as presets, and a way to switch the orientation |

## 8. Architecture, testing, UX and extensibility

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ARC-03 | CI gates: preview equals downscaled export, tiled equals untiled, viewport equals a crop of a full render, per-process-version stability; run on every pull request on Apple Silicon | Do better | P0–P2 | M | EDT-02 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | In progress: four gates are engine tests: viewport equals a crop of a full render, tiled stills equal untiled ones with noise reduction on (verified to catch seams), the preview equals a downscaled export by each filter (ARC-04), and per-process-version stability (212a4d6): five fixtures (Sony ARW, Fujifilm RAF, iPhone ProRAW, Pixel DNG with a HueSatMap, a JPEG) at every process version with a heavy edit, exported at 384 px and rendered at 1:1, kept as patch colours and detail in `tests/golden/process/`, with a second edit for brush, range and AI masks, Heal and Clone spots, crop and Transform, and black and white (763f453), recorded once (only missing versions, `TEST_RUNNER_REDLAMP_RECORD_PROCESS_GOLDEN=1`). Rendering repeats bit for bit, so the limits are tight (mean ΔE2000 0.02, worst 0.2); it caught a 4% change to Clarity's gain and a 6% change to grain. Not done: whether CI's runner has a Metal device (the gates skip without one), and limits measured on other GPUs | [DT §8](darktable-findings.md#8-architecture-performance-and-ux) |
| ARC-04 | Define every filter radius in full-resolution pixels | Adopt | P2 | S | — | Accepted (2026-09-30) | In progress: a CI gate compares the fitted preview with a same-size export (full resolution, then downscaled) by each filter's change in luminance, and must stay within 0.8–1.25x. Clarity and Dehaze matched (0.97–1.00); Texture showed 0.64x at 1000 px of a 6000 px Nikon file and nothing at 700 px, because the preview dropped the octave a downscaled export keeps. It now reads the rendered texels for that octave: 0.99x at 1000 px, 1.10x at 2000 and 0.63x at 700. Still open: grain is about 4x stronger in the preview (drawn at display pixels; with TON-19), and sharpening at small radii is invisible at fit while faintly present in a downscaled export, as in Lightroom; noise reduction has averaged away at that scale in both | [DT §8](darktable-findings.md#8-architecture-performance-and-ux) |
| ARC-05 | Tiled rendering for large exports on iPhone and iPad, each stage declaring its overlap | Adopt | P5 | M | ARC-01 | Proposed | Not started | [DT §8](darktable-findings.md#8-architecture-performance-and-ux) |
| ARC-06 | Benchmark harness and recorded performance history: `redlamp bench` on the CC0 fixtures, the slider sweep and the folders run as JSON, `scripts/perf-record.sh` appending one record per run to `docs/performance/history.jsonl` (noisy under load), seeded from the README's figures in git history, and drawn at redlamp.app/performance | Build | P0 | M | — | Proposed | In progress: the harness, the recorder, the README backfill (19 records since 29 September) and the page are done; the CI regression gates of the Phase 0 performance lab are not started | This tracker |
| ARC-07 | End-to-end regression suite, its foundation: a driver inside a test build of the app (`packages/RedlampAutomation`) that works the app through the user's own input paths (keys, menus, clicks, drags), in a home of its own with no network; contract tests in CI that every action, parameter, panel, tool and feedback feature has a scenario; a supervisor (`mise run e2e`) that catches crashes, hangs and errors and writes a report; the smoke tier; and `mise run release` refusing to ship without a passing run and a black-box check of the signed app | Build | P0 | L | — | Accepted (2026-10-05): the owner approved the plan: an in-process driver, and performance blocking a release only when measured on a quiet Mac | Done (`d4bec74`, `2e6c94f`, `8dae595`, `efa6a8d`, `8ef9b44`, `89933dc`): the driver, compiled into Debug and profiling builds only, works the app from a thread of its own; `mise run e2e` runs a copy as `app.redlamp.mac.e2e` in a home of its own with a stub relay, checks the owner's files and preferences are unchanged, catches crashes, stalls and deadlines, retries once and reports a pass on retry as flaky; 14 contract tests; the smoke tier passes in about 2.5 minutes; `mise run release` runs the release tier or accepts a passing report for the commit, then checks the signed app as a black box. It found ⌘[ and ⌘] doing nothing and Depth Range Mask never available (fixed in `86aa866`). Synthetic ⇧⌘ and ⌥⌘ shortcuts don't run SwiftUI's menu items, so for those the suite checks the binding and runs the item from the menu. Keys from outside the app's process are dropped, recorded and counted in the report, as a context menu takes the keyboard even in the background (17588de); a failure's record gives each activity event's time. Test libraries close their index before their folder goes, so SQLite no longer warns of files unlinked in use and late snapshots and thumbnails leave no folders behind (e08b872); more speed tests take `.measuresSpeed`, and waits run on the condition (ac349d1, c8f2c44); test libraries close through one helper, `LibrarySandbox.remove`, the index closed off the main thread before the folder goes (19b1be7); keys, scrolls and gestures from outside the app's process are dropped in `NSApplication.sendEvent(_:)`, before any of its monitors, its menus' key equivalents or a window sees them, as AppKit calls local monitors in no fixed order and the driver's own monitor had let a key through in 7 of 10 runs (7e6348d); scenario `driver.foreign-keys` (0df29ce); the folder-removal scenarios choose Remove from Folders as a click does, once the menu has closed (66f8819) | This tracker ([design](../plans/2026-10-05-regression-suite-design.md)) |
| ARC-08 | End-to-end regression suite, every feature: scenario packs for every feedback area (Develop, Viewing, Workspace, History, Masking, Crop, Healing, Library, Saving, Sync, Export, Recipes, Focus stacking, Raw, Feedback, Camera bench), a seeded soak tier with replayable failures, and a performance tier with budgets recorded in `docs/performance/history.jsonl`, all in the release gate | Build | P0 | L | ARC-07 | Accepted (2026-10-05): with ARC-07 | Done (`1b0bd30`, `8598688`, `4f9b2bb`, `2049b8e`, `6c55e59`, `f82b695`, `3011f20`, `f4c0a5f`, `097f070`, `0cecfe2`, `519ec0b`, `5e3267e`, `546ef4d`): 63 full-tier scenarios cover 385 of the 412 claims, with 27 exempt for a stated reason; the soak tier walks about 4,000 seeded steps in 5 minutes and replays a failure with `--seed` and `--replay`; the performance tier measures seven `e2e-*` metrics, judged against `tests/e2e/budgets.json` only on a quiet Mac; after each run it checks storage: the test home's caches against the app's limits and nothing left in the temporary folder (`519ec0b`, `5e3267e`, `546ef4d`). The release tier takes about 13 minutes on the M1 Ultra. It found model compiles that filled the disk when several ran at once (fixed in `519ec0b`) and Sync Settings writing sidecars on the main thread (`101f321`). Previous Photo's menu item is sometimes still disabled on a first try and passes on retry | This tracker ([design](../plans/2026-10-05-regression-suite-design.md)) |

Notices are a token type of their own (`NoticeTone`, and `NoticeColors` in every theme's palette): caution in amber whatever the theme, info in the theme's foreground, drawn as a card (`NoticeCard`) with a glyph, its actions and a close button. | AUD-01 | Data safety fixes from audit round 1: exports never replace a photo, in the app or the CLI; a sidecar this build can't read, or couldn't write back without loss, is never deleted or overwritten; emptying a sidecar keeps its history; unknown fields survive at every level; iCloud conflicts resolve only the copies that were merged; failed saves are reported, pending saves flushed on quit, and another writer's changes kept; unchanged sidecars aren't rewritten; interrupted saves and deletes are cleaned up; iCloud merges keep every copy's rating, flag and label; a damaged edit has a way out; a mask shape with several kinds opens read-only (DATA-01 to DATA-15) | Build | P2 | L | — | Accepted (2026-10-03) | Done (b74fafe, d3f9016, 9421806, e02e3ef, 7ae1244, 8786f24): landed in b74fafe: exports never replace a photo, in the app or the CLI; sidecars that can't be read, or that a save would change, open read-only and are never overwritten or deleted; unknown fields kept at every level; emptied sidecars keep their history; iCloud conflicts merged only when every copy can be (DATA-01, 03, 04, 06, 08, 12). Landed in d3f9016: one ordered save queue with a 2 s maximum wait; failed saves shown with Retry, kept per photo and retried; saving before quitting; another writer's changes merged, the other edit kept as a snapshot (DATA-02, 05, 07, 09). Landed in 9421806: damaged gain maps, profile gain table maps and JPEG XL sizes, and any decode-service reply the app can't use, fail that photo instead of crashing or over-allocating (FUZZ-01 to 03, in the decoder and the reply); iCloud merges keep every copy's rating, flag and label (DATA-13); a mask shape with several kinds opens read-only (DATA-15); interrupted saves and deletes leave nothing behind (DATA-11); unchanged sidecars aren't rewritten, already on main (DATA-10). Landed in e02e3ef: focus-stack frames decode through the decoder service like photos, and the app never decodes in its own process (a service that can't start fails the photo); a stack frame that can't be read is left out and can't be a brush's source, the stack's info says how many couldn't be read, and such a stack is merged once and kept until one of them can be read, rather than on every open, export and stroke; a raw whose preview's offset and length overflow is refused instead of crashing the app (FUZZ-01, FUZZ-02). Landed in 7ae1244: a rating, flag or label, Auto Sync, pasting onto a selection, Update AI Masks and Heal Dust never write over a sidecar that couldn't be read; a read that fails for now is retried like other failed saves, an edit that doesn't decode is reported, a batch skips and reports the photo, and Undo Sync says which photos it couldn't put back (DATA-18 outside the editor, under DATA-01). Landed in 8786f24: a photo whose sidecar can't be read opens read-only and is read again until it can be, never unedited, and work started meanwhile can't land on its edit (DATA-18); a damaged edit is called damaged, and Start Over keeps it in the sidecar beside a new edit (DATA-14); a save that keeps failing keeps every visit's history and shows another writer's edit merged. What the app still parses itself is DATA-17, waiting for review | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Data safety plan |
| AUD-02 | Responsiveness fixes from audit round 1: drawing or selecting a mask no longer rebuilds the whole Masking panel; a photo's embedded mattes are read once per session, off the main thread; switching to an already decoded photo updates the UI once, without blocking the main thread (RESP-02 to RESP-04) | Build | P2 | M | AUD-01 | Accepted (2026-10-03) | Done (1cdf47c, 65bd110, 4a88275): the Masking panel keeps its rows (drawing and selecting still cost 50–87 ms, RESP-13); embedded mattes are read once per photo, off the main thread; switching to a photo decoded ahead changes the editor once, when its edit has been read, so its first frame comes in about 40 ms instead of 125–140 ms, with mostly 20–30 ms of main-thread time instead of 180–195 ms (measured at a load average of about 50). While that edit is read, the photo left takes no edits, ratings, flags, labels and Previous and Next go to the photo being opened, and going straight back starts a new visit; past 100 ms the new photo's thumbnail shows, as before (RESP-02 to RESP-04) | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Responsiveness plan |
| AUD-03 | Memory fixes from audit round 1: the detail stage's scratch textures and idle cached outputs are purgeable between renders, and its working set is sized to the current photo instead of the largest view ever rendered (MEM-01) | Build | P2 | M | — | Accepted (2026-10-03) | Done (ec098fd; 5785c16 keeps a new command buffer at a freed one's address from being taken for it): the detail stage holds 1.3 GB instead of 3.1 GB at 24 MP 1:1 and 2.3 GB instead of 7.5 GB at 60 MP, keeps nothing when idle, and tiles large views within its budget; output unchanged | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Memory plan |
| AUD-04 | Concurrency fixes from audit round 1: Auto Tone, Upright, Auto White Balance, the eyedropper and Apply Recipe apply their result only to the photo they were computed for; exporting another photo no longer evicts the open photo's mask data (CONC-03, CONC-04) | Build | P2 | S | AUD-01 | Accepted (2026-10-03) | Done (0d0ef26, d5eada3, b3538ee): exporting another photo no longer evicts the open photo's mask data (masked frames during an export 2.4 ms instead of 62 ms; kept rasters capped at 128 MB a photo and 256 MB in all, purgeable); analysis results land only on the photo they were computed for, at all 25 places that apply one, and Remove Dust across a selection heals the open photo in the editor (CONC-03, CONC-04) | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Concurrency plan |
| AUD-05 | Render pipeline fixes from audit round 1: slider and brush drags render only the visible area; the noise-reduction pass is cached on its own, so Sharpening, Texture and Clarity drags don't recompute it (PIPE-01, PIPE-03) | Build | P2 | M | AUD-03 | Accepted (2026-10-03) | Done (0123344, a6fff29): landed in 0123344: before process 11, Sharpening, Texture and Clarity drags keep the noise-reduced image instead of denoising again (about 13–17 ms to 5–7 ms a frame), a Noise drag reuses the image it replaces, and from any process a mask's Texture, Clarity or Sharpness drag no longer reruns noise reduction; output unchanged (PIPE-03). Also fixed: from process 11, with Luminance at 0, adding a mask drew interactive frames from the detail ladder computed without it (PIPE-13). Landed in a6fff29: while a slider, palette or scroll edit is in progress at zoom, only the visible area plus a 64-pixel guard is rendered, and the margin once it ends, so a Luminance drag at 1:1 takes about 30 ms a frame instead of 94 and a Sharpen drag 9 instead of 28 (an 1800 × 1100 window, at a load average of about 72); a pan reuses the overview and histogram; output unchanged (PIPE-01). PIPE-16, found on the way, waits for the owner | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Render pipeline plan |

## 9. AI denoise and focus stacking (product priorities)

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DN-01 | Noise profiles: DNG NoiseProfile tag, calibrated per-camera profiles, blind estimation; calibration tool and the first 10 bodies | Build | P2 | 5 ew | — | Accepted (2026-09-30) | In progress: the DNG NoiseProfile tag is read, otherwise noise is measured blind at open (same-colour sample differences in 16 px tiles, the 5th percentile per brightness, a weighted Poisson–Gaussian fit; about 30 ms). Busy scenes in lossy-compressed raws still read up to about 3× too noisy (see DN-10). The calibrated profiles and their tool are done (05a8cdd): `redlamp noise calibrate` fits each ISO by photon transfer from pairs of frames shot alike (each pair's difference cancels the scene and fixed-pattern noise; a weighted line per colour through 64 px tiles), profiles ship in RedlampServices' resources and are interpolated across ISO on log scales, at most a stop past the calibrated ones, and the cascade is the DNG tag, then the camera's profile, then the measurement, which wins when a raw is far cleaner than its profile (noise reduction in camera). `redlamp noise check` shows which applies. The first 10 bodies wait on calibration frames (DN-05) | [AI §2.2](ai-findings.md#22-noise-profiles-and-calibration), [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| DN-02 | Classical NR v1: luma pyramid shrinkage, luma-guided chroma, HQ non-local means pass, Lightroom Detail-panel mapping | Build | P2 | 8–11 ew | DN-01, DEC-05 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (commit "Non-local means for luma at 1:1 and in exports"): v1 renders behind the Detail panel's six noise sliders. Five-scale à-trous B3 shrinkage (non-negative garrote, chroma shrunk as a vector) in a noise-stabilised (generalised Anscombe) opponent space, with each scale's noise measured through the real demosaic and pyramid. Dragging a noise slider at 1:1 renders in 9–10 ms (p95 14 ms). Chroma follows luma edges (Lee's sigma filter, 1983, across channels: a neighbour's chroma counts as much as its luma resembles the centre's within three of the level's noise sigmas), so colour no longer bleeds across them: around a clipped orange light on a blue ground at Color 100, the colour cast falls by 90% (0.0284 to 0.0029 at 4x noise), with PSNR, texture and flat chroma unchanged. Leaving clipped samples out of the filter (Foi 2009) was tried and dropped: here noise reduction runs after highlight reconstruction, so clipped samples are already plausible, and excluding them made a coloured light's cast slightly worse. Luma keeps more detail by shrinking each coefficient by its 3×3 neighbourhood's energy at that scale rather than by its own size (Lee's local statistics, 1980), so faint texture beside real texture survives: Luminance 50 now gains 2.2 dB at 2x noise and 3.0 dB at 4x (from 1.0 and 1.65), about 1 dB more than before at the same texture retained. The extra pass costs about 1 ms of GPU time on a 2560 × 1600 view at 1:1 and runs only when luma is being reduced; Color alone and sharpening's separator (which keeps the plain garrote it was calibrated with) cost what they did. The HQ pass is in: at 1:1 and in exports (pyramid level 0) with Luminance on, luma becomes non-local means (DEC-21) of the noisy texels, weighted by how alike the wavelet result's 3×3 patches are within 7×7. Guiding by the wavelet result rather than the noisy image is what makes it work: Luminance 50 now gains 3.0 dB at 2x noise and 4.1 dB at 4x, keeping 90% and 72% of the texture (the wavelet alone: 2.2 and 3.0 dB, 85% and 61%), and Luminance 100 keeps 81% where it kept 70%. 5×5 patches, 11×11 windows and keeping some of the wavelet's luma were all worse or no better. Tiled in threadgroup memory with a 2×4 block of texels per thread, it adds about 1.3 ms of GPU time to a 2560 × 1600 view at 1:1 (5.5 ms in its first, per-texel form). v1 uses only techniques published before 1996, so DEC-05 gates only those later pieces (non-local means is cleared by DEC-21). A benchmark (`NoiseBenchmarkTests`: dead leaves, a flat patch and a clipped highlight with exact Poisson–Gaussian noise and a matching profile, scored against the noise-free pipeline) gives the baseline: Luminance 50 gains 1.0 dB at 2x noise and 1.65 dB at 4x, keeping 86% and 62% of the texture; Color 25 cuts flat chroma error by 60% | [AI §2.1](ai-findings.md#21-classical-denoiser-nr-v1) |
| DN-03 | Sensor cleanup stage: hot pixels, banding, color bias | Build | P2 | 2 ew | DN-01 | Proposed | In progress: hot pixels are repaired before demosaicing (8 noise sigmas and 2× above every neighbour within two pixels, other colours included); no false repairs on the six sample cameras. Banding: row and column offsets are measured in the optical-black margins (LibRaw's left and top margins, used only when at least 16 photosites wide and at the black level), with outliers clipped at 5 sigmas and each line's estimate shrunk by the share of its variance that exceeds noise, and subtracted on the GPU with the black level. Of the sample cameras only the Canon R6 has masked margins; at ISO 100 its rows band by 0.17 DN RMS (1.3 DN pixel noise), and that is corrected; the Fujifilm's top rows and the Sony's extra columns aren't at the black level, so are left alone. Colour bias not started | [AI §2.1](ai-findings.md#21-classical-denoiser-nr-v1) |
| DN-04 | Core ML timing on an A17 Pro iPhone for NAFNet raw variants and SAM 2.1 | Adopt | P5 | S | — | Proposed | Not started. First compare the same filter placed after the demosaic: the literature finds the order alone isn't what helps, but splitting denoising across it. Measured as a prototype: 0.4 to 1.2 dB over Redlamp's best setting on photos at high ISO, 1.2 to 5.8 dB on fine text, a third of the chroma noise. The filmstrip offers "Focus stack detected: N frames" with Merge, which saves a `.redlampstack` beside the frames; runs a document already covers aren't offered again. Finds the public R5 II stack and nothing among the 40 look-development raws or the fixtures (0.4 s for 40 files, warm). Vendor maker-note tags not started: LibRaw's maker-note callback passes a C++ stream, so it needs a small C++ shim | [AI §3.2](ai-findings.md#32-stack-detection); [Katami §5](notes/LIB-katami.md#5-proposed-tracker-changes) |
| FS-02 | Focus stacking v1: GPU alignment, focus measure, depth solve, streaming Auto/Smooth/Detail fusion, artifacts | Build | P3 | 9–11 ew | CAM-05, LNS-01, DN-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30): the full feature, developed on public raw stacks; frames are stacked as demosaiced camera RGB before any edit, so the result develops like a raw; a separate Stack workspace | In progress: alignment (ECC similarity on the CPU, chained from the narrowest view, Lanczos GPU warp, per-channel gains), the depth solve (sharpness measured in each frame's own geometry and then warped, at a quarter of a 2048-pixel analysis copy) and streaming Auto/Smooth/Detail fusion are done, with `redlamp stack`. On 25 frames of a public Canon R5 II series (every 40th of 999, CC BY 4.0): 8.1 s at 5118×3412 (decode 4.6 s with three frames decoding ahead of the GPU, align 3.0 s, depth 0.2 s, fusion 0.3 s; was 24 s), 2.3 GB peak (was 4.5); this smooth, low-texture surface leaves alignment poorly constrained (two ECC schedules with equal correlation differ by up to 300 px at the corners yet merge equally well), so alignment accuracy still needs a textured public raw stack. On the PoC's textured JPEG stacks: PCB, 7 frames (MIT): 3.6% breathing and 91% confident depth (PoC 3.7%, 91%) in 1.0 s (PoC 5.2 s); against `focus-stack`'s own result put through the same default develop, the gain over the sharpest frame matches the PoC (Smooth +8.4 dB, Auto +6.8, Detail +6.8; PoC +8.4, +6.3, +5.3). Mite in amber, 109 frames (CC BY 4.0): 65% confident depth (PoC 63%) in 14.6 s (PoC 97 s), 4.7 GB peak (was 10.5 before releasing each frame's GPU objects as it finishes); as sharp as the Python PoC at 1600 px (Tenengrad over the best frame: Auto 1.42 vs 1.35, Detail 1.96 vs 1.94, Smooth 1.39 vs 1.37). Depth artifacts: brightness is matched before measuring sharpness (focus breathing changes exposure; the brightest frame used to win wherever nothing was sharp: 8.8% of the defocused corner, now 0.02%), and where neither solve is confident the depth is filled from its surroundings (push-pull), so Smooth no longer blotches flat backgrounds; finally the depth map is smoothed along the image (guided by the sharpest-frame luma), so multi-peaked focus in transparent subjects (the mite in amber) no longer leaves depth-label seams in Smooth, at a small cost on the PCB (Smooth 24.09 dB and SSIM 0.866 against `focus-stack`, was 24.13 and 0.873, about 9% softer by Tenengrad; Auto and Detail unchanged). Clipped highlights count for nothing in the focus measure (a blown specular has the hardest edges in whichever frame clipped it; PCB Auto 22.60 dB and Smooth 24.17 dB against `focus-stack`, were 22.55 and 24.09). Lens corrections, the feature-matching fallback and halo handling beyond the Auto window not started | [AI §3.3](ai-findings.md#33-classical-pipeline-and-defaults) |

## 10. Other AI workstreams

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |

| RM-01 | Classical Heal and Clone (Poisson blending, exhaustive GPU source search, no PatchMatch) | Build | P3 | 5–7 ew | — | Accepted (2026-10-02): next in the plan the owner approved | Done: Heal and Clone spots render into a copy of the photo before everything else, saved with the edit and in Copy Settings (6b91624). Heal is Poisson blending solved without an iterative solver: the rim's log ratio of destination to source weighed by 1/d², which is the Poisson integral on a disc, after a median along the rim so a twig or wire crossing it doesn't bleed in. Each spot finds its own source (4916700): every place within ten radii, on the pyramid level where the spot is 8 texels across, matched on the rim and on the strength of its texture (a median absolute deviation, so thin things crossing the rim don't count), so it neither copies a flat area into texture nor takes another blemish. The Healing tool (`Q`, 6a6e488) adds a circle with a click and brushes a spot with a drag, moves spots and sources, resizes them, and sets Heal or Clone, Size, Feather and Opacity; `redlamp render --heal` and `--heal-brush` add them from the command line. A drag brushes a spot along a scratch, wire or twig, weighed over its outline the same way (0490993) | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |
| RM-02 | Dust detection and removal, Visualize Spots | Build | P3 | 3–4 ew | — | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner asked for dust, specks and distraction removal | Done (b3fb8cc): Remove Dust (bb8a8d9) in the Healing tool finds sensor dust and heals each speck with an automatic source, in one step (and `redlamp render --remove-dust`). Specks are differences of Gaussians on log luminance at three scales, standing out by several times their neighbourhood's spread; dust is kept only where the photo is as smooth as its noise (the response noise alone would give), round, soft (its half depth well inside its tenth, unlike a pit or a stone), equally dark in every channel, with nothing dark crossing its surroundings and few specks near it. On the six sample raws only four dark spots on the Sony's smooth sandstone are found; dust added to the iPhone sample's sky is all found. Visualize Spots shows log luminance against a blur 8 times wider, in white on black, at a sensitivity. Across a shoot (66a9491): with several photos selected, Remove Dust looks at each (a little more keenly than for one photo), keeps the specks found at the same sensor place and about the same size in at least two of them and a fifth of the selection, and heals them in every photo from that sensor, the open one as a step of its own history and the rest as a batch Undo puts back. Evaluated (b3fb8cc) in place of a dusty sensor, which the owner doesn't have: realistic dust (shadows 3 to 12 pixels wide, 4% to 20% deep, a fifth of them fibres) multiplied into the raw data of 13 photos from 13 cameras, a shoot built from them binned to one sensor size, and six frames of a Nikon Z 6 on a tripod (`DustEvaluationTests`, samples by `mise run fixtures-shoots`). It found that a big speck raised its own bar (its neighbourhood's spread is now measured beyond its own reach), that fabric knots and marks in wood passed for dust (a speck with round look-alikes nearby, as dark, is the scene's), and that a tripod repeats the scene's specks as faithfully as dust (frames showing the same textured scene around a speck now count once). 12 of 16 visible round specks are found (11 before) with 0.38 false finds a frame (0.77); specks found in enough frames reach every frame; the Z 6's own dust is found in all six. Fibres mostly pass unfound (1 of 3), not being round | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |

| RM-07 | Remove brush with content-aware fill: exemplar-based inpainting (Criminisi, Pérez and Toyama, 2004; patents expired) coarse to fine with an exhaustive GPU patch search (not PatchMatch), blended with Heal's Poisson seams; for litter, signs, wires and specks on natural texture | Build | P3 | L | RM-01 | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner chose classical removal now, a learned inpainter later | Done (6d6260d): Remove spots, circles or brushed, in the Healing tool and `redlamp render --remove`. Each hole is filled on the pyramid level where it's at most 96 texels across, one 9 × 9 patch at a time in Criminisi's priority order (confidence times the isophote across the front), each patch the best of every candidate around the hole scored on the GPU; at full size each texel copies from where its patches came from, the four nearest blended, and Heal's rim blending matches the edge to the photo. Fills are cached per spot and the spots before it. On the samples, litter in grass disappears and a pot on a roof becomes roof with its seams continued. Large areas are better served by RM-10. Process 10 (b0f9656) makes Highlights and Shadows', Clarity's, Dehaze's and glow's maps again from the retouched photo, in the background for frames (about 0.1 s for 24 MP) and before rendering for stills and exports, so a removed object leaves no faint outline where they're used; edits from earlier processes keep the photo's | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |

Nothing ranks distractions automatically (US9665962B2 runs to 2035) | Build | P3 | M | RM-07, DEC-24 | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner chose classical removal now | Done (a7042d2): in the Healing tool (384b23f), Click picks Person or Object; a click asks for the people masks (Vision) or an Objects mask at the point (Segment Anything 2.1) and adds a Remove spot shaped by that mask, grown by its radius (0.5% of the height to begin with) and feathered, filled as RM-07 fills. The mask is saved with the edit like an AI mask's. Things named in words: OWLv2 B/16 (Apache-2.0) is converted to Core ML (66124d1), computing in float32 (in float16 a few dozen patches a photo drift through its massive activations, and 8-bit weights move some boxes by over half the square), at 150 ms a photo on the GPU and matching PyTorch on 40 renders; the text features of 19 prompts for 13 things (the owner's list) are precomputed, so no text encoder ships. It is published as the prerelease models-owlv2-base-v1 (2ef16c9; never the app's Latest) and offered in Settings › Models under DEC-24, whose lineage verdict the licence gate now has. In the Healing tool, Find (a7042d2) outlines what OWLv2 finds (one thing or all 13), and a click on one asks Segment Anything 2.1 for its mask from its box (SAM's box prompt) and removes it with a Remove spot, as a picked object; Remove All removes everything outlined as one step, and `redlamp render --remove-found car` does it from the command line. On the D7500 sample the red car is found where it's parked (0.41) and its box gives SAM the whole car. Filled by RM-07, something as large as a car leaves a smeared patch: areas that size are RM-10's | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |
