Every open-vocabulary detector with permissive weights (Florence-2, OWLv2, Grounding DINO, Falcon Perception) has research-only data in its lineage, see DEC-24 | [open-model findings](open-model-findings.md) |
| RM-10 | Opt-in generative fill on Macs: FLUX.2 [klein] 4B (Apache-2.0) through MLX, removal only, about a 2.4 GB download (the transformer and VAE; removal prompts precomputed, so no text encoder ships), for regions too large for RM-07, labelled as generated (RM-03) | Adopt | P3 | M | DEC-23, RM-07 | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner said yes to DEC-23; (2026-10-05) removal only, without the text encoder | Done (2026-10-06, 5ba126b): the model is published as the prerelease models-flux2-klein-4b-fill-v1, once the owner approved the upload (DEC-26); its eight files download from there and match the manifest, so the Healing tool offers Generative on Macs with 16 GB or more, and Report a Bug lists Generative Remove. Step 1 (4512f76): mlx-swift (DEC-25) in a Mac-only RedlampGenerative module, with Qwen3-4B's first 27 layers making FLUX.2 [klein]'s prompt embeddings as transformers does (within 0.1% in float32). Step 2 (e0d4e6d): the transformer, the VAE, the schedule and Flux2KleinInpaintPipeline's loop in MLX, matching diffusers in float32 from the same noise (whole fill above 40 dB on crops of two CC0 photos). On an M1 Ultra at 512 px and 4 steps, 4-bit weights (2.22 GB) fill in 8 to 10 s, as bfloat16 (7.75 GB) and 8-bit (4.15 GB) do, and stay 41 to 46 dB from float32 inside the mask, indistinguishable by eye; 4-bit ships, about 4.6 GB in use while filling. The pipeline passes the photo, the thing to remove included, as a reference, so Generative Remove hides it first. Step 3 (a1b545e): the download, 2.41 GB (4-bit transformer in shards under 2 GB, the VAE, three removal prompts encoded by diffusers' encode_prompt), filling exactly as the model quantised at load does; the model store resumes interrupted downloads and reports progress within a file; a manifest's minimum memory (16 GB until measured) keeps it from smaller Macs; the licence gate takes undisclosed data under DEC-23 only, in CI. Step 4 (5172060): Generative Remove in the engine: a Remove spot's fill kept with the edit as the photo's camera RGB in a 16-bit PNG, rendered with the photo's noise and Heal's rim blending, the same on any Mac, and ignored by builds that don't know it; the model reaches the engine through a protocol, so the engine and iOS never link MLX. What the model is shown decides the result: on the D7500's car the photo itself brings the car back and no reference invents another, while the spot filled from the photo, prompted to remove, gives a clean driveway on three seeds, the default ([design](../plans/2026-10-05-generative-fill-design.md)). On water some of content-aware fill's streaks survive. Step 5 (8172c08): the Healing tool: Remove's Fill is Content-Aware or Generative, shown only where the model is offered, and the first use offers the download with its size, licence and that fills are labelled as generated; a new Remove spot, clicked, brushed, picked or found, gets three fills (Remove All one each) with progress and Cancel, arrows between them, More, and Content-Aware to go back; a filled spot is labelled Generated on the canvas and in the panel, and its steps in History; moving or resizing it drops the fill, which no longer fits, and fills it again; closing the tool lets the model's memory go. Step 6 (13f033d): on the M1 Ultra a fill takes 11 s at 512 pixels, 24 s at 768 and 46 s at 1024, with the model loaded in 3 s; the car, in a Release build, 40 s. A 1024-pixel fill peaked at 13.1 GB, nearly all of it the VAE's convolutions, which MLX unfolds into a copy per kernel tap: convolving large inputs in bands and fusing the norms with SiLU brings it to 6.6 GB with the same fills as diffusers, and capping MLX's cache keeps what the app holds near that, for 7% more time. The minimum stays 16 GB, now measured ([design](../plans/2026-10-05-generative-fill-design.md#what-a-fill-costs)). Fill times on other Macs are still to measure | [open-model findings](open-model-findings.md) |
| RM-11 | Content-aware fill copies only from outside every Remove spot in the edit, so removing several things at once (Find's Remove All, `--remove-found`) doesn't copy one into another's hole: today a spot's fill reads the photo with the spots after it still in, and the duck sample's fills carry other ducks' heads | Build | P3 | S | RM-07 | Accepted (2026-10-04): the owner chose fill quality before generative fill | Done (c43d186): from process 12 a Remove spot's fill copies from none of the edit's other Remove spots and none of the spots after it, isn't matched on what those after it replace, and is made again when a spot after it changes within its window. On the duck sample the teal's bill no longer turns up in the mallard's fill; most of what's left there comes from feather tips and spray just outside the masks, and from RM-12's blocks | Found on the look-dev duck sample (Canon EOS 7D Mark II) while fixing RM-07's trace (b0f9656) |
| RM-12 | Content-aware fill refined coarse to fine: the moves found on the working level are searched again, exhaustively in a small window around themselves, on each finer level (hierarchical block matching, not PatchMatch), so the fill keeps the photo's texture at full size instead of blocks of the working level and the doubled copies of blending four of them; on water the blocks show as vertical streaks | Build | P3 | M | RM-07 | Accepted (2026-10-04): the owner chose fill quality before generative fill | Not started. Tried and not shipped (2026-10-04): searching the working level's moves again on finer levels (each texel's own and its four neighbours' moves, a texel either way, and neighbours up to 16 texels away) keeps the working level's blocks, since they're where each coarse patch copies from, and softened the duck sample's fill. A car a third of the frame across copies sky, doors and walls into the drive at any mask growth (0.5 to 2.5% of the height): holes that size need RM-10. Tried and not shipped (2026-10-05): a finer working level, made affordable by keeping the front's priorities in a heap updated only near each patch (81ce156, which fills exactly as before and ships): with holes up to 160 or 256 texels across instead of 96 the duck sample's water still shows the mallard's vertical ghost and copies of the other duck, and at 1:1 its texture is the same, for 1.5 to 2.6 times the time, so no process version. What's left is the source: water near the hole is the ducks' reflections, which RM-13's reflection regions would take out of the candidates; and a wider search on finer levels, checked against DEC-05 | Found on the look-dev duck sample (Canon EOS 7D Mark II) while fixing RM-07's trace (b0f9656) |
| RM-13 | A picked person or object's cast shadow and reflection removed with it: a classical first pass (the region beside the mask darker than its surroundings in the same colour, and on water the mirror image below it), then open instance-shadow models checked under DEC-24 | Build | P3 | M | RM-08 | Accepted (2026-10-05): the owner chose it for the masking plan | Done (2026-10-05, 2b186e7): a person or object picked with a click, or removed from Find or `--remove-found`, takes its cast shadow and its reflection with it, under the Healing tool's Shadows and reflections checkbox (on by default; `--keep-shadows` in the CLI). The shadow is ground touching the thing from its lowest quarter down, darker than 0.6 of the ground around it in the ground's colour or a cooler one (lit by the sky alone), and bordering lit ground along most of its edge. It is bounded to a quarter of the thing's width beyond either end and a third of its size below it, since shade from something else that it touches is as dark and as blue: the D7500 car sample's front shadow goes and the house's shade beside the car stays. The reflection is the thing mirrored below its lowest point, kept as deep as the photo there takes on the thing's colour against what lies beside it, rippled or not: three of the look-dev duck sample's four ducks lose theirs, and the fourth's pale reflection stays. Both are found on a grid at most 1024 pixels across. Telling a thing's own shadow from shade it touches: RM-14 | Found on the look-dev duck sample (Canon EOS 7D Mark II) while fixing RM-07's trace (b0f9656) |
| RM-14 | A removed thing's own cast shadow told apart from shade it only touches (a wall's, a tree's), which RM-13's classical pass can only bound: open instance-shadow models, which pair each object with its shadow, checked under DEC-24, or the shadow's shape matched to the thing's outline and the light's direction | Adopt | P3 | M | RM-13, DEC-24 | Proposed | Not started | Split from RM-13 (2026-10-05): on the D7500 car sample the house's shade beside the car is as dark and as blue as the car's own shadow |
| RM-15 | Generative Remove on Macs with less than 16 GB: offered there with a note that it hasn't been tested on Macs with less memory and may be slow or not work, instead of kept from them; measuring fill time, memory pressure and swap on an 8 GB Mac at 512 and 768 pixels, and capping crops there if that shows a need, waits for one. A 1024-pixel fill peaks at 6.6 GB on the M1 Ultra, a 512-pixel one at 4.2 GB | Adopt | P3 | S | RM-10 | Accepted (2026-10-07): the owner has no Mac with less than 16 GB, so smaller Macs are offered it with a note that it hasn't been tested on them | Done (commit "Generative Remove on Macs with less than 16 GB, with a note that it hasn't been tested on them"): the model's manifest names the memory it was tested on (`testedMemory`, 16 GB) instead of a minimum, so smaller Macs are offered it; on one, the Healing tool's download offer, Settings › Models and a failed fill's message say it hasn't been tested on Macs with less memory and may be slow or not work there, and `redlamp render --generative` warns. Not measured: what a fill costs on an 8 GB Mac | [RR §5.1](rapidraw-findings.md#51-generative-remove-on-8-gb-macs-lesson-1) |
| RM-16 | Fills from another Mac on the local network: a `GenerativeFiller` that sends a Remove spot's crop to a Mac running Redlamp (the app or `redlamp serve`), found over Bonjour and paired once, which fills it with the same model, steps and seeds; the fill keeps its Generated label and records the server; iPad and iPhone use it in Phase 5 | Do better | P3, P5 | M | DEC-37, RM-10 | Rejected (2026-10-07): the owner won't pursue it, as too hard for most photographers to set up; cloud providers instead (DEC-39) | Not started | [RR §5.2](rapidraw-findings.md#52-fills-from-a-server-the-photographer-runs-lesson-2) |
| RM-17 | ComfyUI as a fill server wherever the photographer runs it, on their network or hosted in the cloud (one of DEC-39's routes), through ComfyUI's own HTTP and WebSocket API (upload, queue, progress, fetch, interrupt) with no middleware: its address and any key in Settings, workflows as templates whose named inputs (image, mask, reference, seed, prompt) are checked on import, templates shipped only for models whose weights allow commercial use, and ComfyUI (GPL-3.0) never bundled or linked; the fill records its workflow and model | Adopt | Later | M | DEC-39, RM-10 | Proposed | Not started: which route comes first waits on INF-11, whose [ComfyUI note](notes/INF-11-comfyui-masks-denoise.md) covers this route | [RR §5.2](rapidraw-findings.md#52-fills-from-a-server-the-photographer-runs-lesson-2) |

| SHP-01 | Noise-aware capture sharpening: NR v1 separates the noise, a few Richardson–Lucy or Wiener iterations deconvolve the clean estimate (Gaussian PSF from Radius), and the residual is added back with a "keep noise" amount | Build | P2 | M (2–3 ew) | TON-06, DN-02 | Accepted (2026-09-30): process version 1, Lightroom's four sliders, no "keep noise" control (noise always passes through) | Done (commit "Noise-aware capture sharpening"). The separator is the denoiser at 3 sigmas on luma and chroma (luma alone let flat noise grow 17–29%); 4 Richardson–Lucy iterations; Detail blends unsharp mask and deconvolution. Calibrated in `shp01_calibrate.py`: at Radius 2 / Amount 100 / Detail 100, +1.7 dB on mild softness and +1.2 dB on noisy defocus, where the old unsharp mask lost 0.9 dB; flat noise changes under 2%. The analysis is cached per area and Radius, so dragging Amount, Detail, Masking or noise sliders at 1:1 on a 5K display costs about 1 ms more than noise reduction alone (13–15.5 ms per frame); dragging Radius stays slower (29–30 ms p50 after precomputed blur weights and half-float deconvolution images; fusing each iteration into one tiled kernel was exact but twice as slow) | [H §6.2–6.3](notes/H-topaz-upscale-sharpen.md#62-sharpening-and-deblur) |

## 13. Library and catalog

The library and catalog track (5 October 2026): folders on disk organise photos, each photo's metadata stays in its sidecar, and an index on the Mac keeps a library of a million photos instant on local, spinning and network disks (DEC-42 to DEC-49). LIB-01 to LIB-04 come first: the research study, the measured design and the stress harness, whose budgets gate every merge. The core ships in 1.0; LIB-31 to LIB-35 and LIB-43 come after it. The research (LIB-01) refined the rows, split exact duplicates (LIB-39) from LIB-31 and added LIB-36 to LIB-38; the owner accepted its changes on 5 October 2026. A study of Katami ([LIB-katami](notes/LIB-katami.md), 6 October 2026) added Library Health (LIB-40), moments and grouping (LIB-41), soft frames in bursts without a model (LIB-42, split from OTH-02, its phase waiting on a measurement against the owner's own culls), sensor dust followed across shoots (LIB-43) and Open With from Finder (UX-37), and reworded LIB-06, LIB-18, LIB-23, LIB-26, LIB-34, LIB-38, OTH-02 and FS-01; the owner asked for it to be folded into the catalog work the same day. A study of Cling, a fuzzy file finder for the Mac ([LIB-cling](notes/LIB-cling.md), 7 October 2026), added the column store mapped from a snapshot (LIB-44) and text that ignores accents and width everywhere (DEC-52), and reworded LIB-06, LIB-18 and LIB-19; the owner accepted its changes the same day.

Not done: the 1M and 2M fixtures, cold-cache runs (they need the owner's terminal), a real network share | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| LIB-04 | Library benchmarks and regression gates: `redlamp library bench` (a cold index build, a warm launch, reconciling after changes, search as you type, facets and sorts, bulk writes, an interrupted rename, a volume vanishing) and `--library-perf` in the app (grid scrolling, held arrow keys in every view, switching modules, culling 10,000 photos, files arriving, memory through each phase), each with a PASS/FAIL budget, differential checks of query results, and metrics on redlamp.app/performance | Build | P4 | L | LIB-03 | Accepted (2026-10-05): the owner's request | In progress: `redlamp library bench` with PASS/FAIL budgets (listing, the fixture check, index-build, warm-launch, reconcile, vanishing-volume, search, facets, store, lists, naming, duplicates) and the app's `--library-perf` (062a896, c28d4dd: warm launch, a folder in the filmstrip, held arrow keys, memory), on library/catalog; `bench --index` keeps a built index between runs, and `library-launch` now measures the library visible and searchable, with reconciling apart. Not done: `--library-perf` on 20,000 photos on a quiet disk, the metrics on redlamp.app/performance | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| LIB-05 | Library index: macOS's own SQLite (no package) in RedlampDocument, with a schema and migrations, WAL with one writer and read connections, never on the main thread and always on the Mac's own disk; snapshots and integrity checks while it runs; rebuildable from the photos and their sidecars at any time | Build | P4 | L | DEC-42, LIB-02 | Accepted (2026-10-05): in the core the owner chose for 1.0 (DEC-46) | In progress: built on library/catalog (d8fcf5c to d00f749; schema version 2 with LIB-06): at a million photos a path lookup in 8 µs and a text search in 0.2 ms. A root being removed is marked in the settings table (`library.removing.<id>`), so reads leave its photos out before its rows are swept, without a schema change (2265afb). Photo, folder and root IDs are never given twice, from counters in the settings table, so a batch Undo, which writes sidecars by photo ID, can't reach a photo added after a removal (cdca918). Collection, keyword, camera and lens IDs never repeat either, and the last IDs are kept beside the index in `Index.ids`, synced in the transaction that gives them, so an index restored from a snapshot or rebuilt from nothing gives none again (e34837a, 28633ac, c94f23c); Put Back finds collections by path and reads again a photo whose camera or lens the index doesn't have, and a sidecar move resumes by its root's path. The writer's commits don't checkpoint: a connection of its own does, keeping the full syncs, and a write waits only once the log reaches 32,768 pages (edbf7e3); schema version 8 adds an index on the photos with sidecars by folder (076d19b); syncing `Index.ids` costs about 10% of an index build at a million (1.77 ms per 1,000-photo transaction); since, `Index.ids` is reserved a block ahead (65,536 photos, 4,096 folders, 256 for the rest), synced only when an ID passes the mark and settled 2 s after the last write, so the build at a million is 12% faster and a crash skips at most a block per table (8b1e818); the text index is merged in steps of about 8 ms after writes of text, FTS5's automerge off for every writer and a write returning once no level holds 8 segments (2401e5a, b16e7bc): with keyword batches running another write waits p99 54 ms from 1,284, search staying at p95 0.1 to 0.6 ms; and checkpoints run every 256 pages, halving the long waits a checkpoint's sync causes (bdc9e6d). The library stops its weekly check when it closes, which had run 60 s later on the closed index and failed with a disk I/O error (e27bd1e). Done when it reaches main | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| LIB-06 | Query engine for a million photos: one query language for the filter bar, the command palette, smart collections and the command line, whose text and rule-editor forms convert into each other (empty fields, relative dates, distance, AND, OR and EXCEPT, ranges, nested groups, missing and offline photos), compiled to SQLite with an FTS5 text index (prefix and substring), and in-memory columns and bitmaps for filters, sorts and facet counts where SQLite alone misses the budget; traits, named shorthands that are each a query over indexed fields, offered in completion with counts: Long Exposure (a second or more), Panorama (2:1 or wider), High Resolution, Low Light (ISO 3200 or more) and No Location, with Wide Open, Telephoto and Ultra Wide once the index keeps the lens's widest aperture and the 35 mm-equivalent focal length, and Photos with Text, One Person, Two People, Groups and Smiling with LIB-32 and LIB-34; free text under three characters still matched against folders, cameras, lenses, creators, places and keyword synonyms, only the trigram index's part left out; text ignores accents and width everywhere (DEC-52) | Build | P4 | L | LIB-05 | Accepted (2026-10-05): in the core the owner chose for 1.0 (DEC-46) | In progress: built on library/catalog (8 commits, merged in ecc47d0): at a million photos the first page and count in p95 2.1 ms (budget 16 ms) and facets in p95 11 ms (budget 100 ms), 70 bytes a photo; every count on lib-20k equals the manifest's. For the filter bar (eddf220): `missing:` and `offline:`, sorts by modification date and file size, counts per metadata column, filtered lists, a rule form and completions; the column store 9 bytes a photo larger. Custom labels (and a label set's names for their colours), `collection:`, IPTC Core's creator, copyright and location as filters, `has:` and free text, matched against small tables of names so indexing isn't slower; traits `is:long-exposure`, `is:panorama`, `is:high-resolution`, `is:low-light` and `is:no-location`, with `megapixels:` and `aspect:`, offered in completion with counts (e922380 to d8e17b9). At a million photos search stays at p95 2.5 ms and facets at 6.0 to 10.6 ms; the column store is 87.5 bytes a photo and loads in 459 to 634 ms (from 323 to 337). Text beyond ASCII folded once and matched by bytes: a keystroke over 5,604 accented folders in p95 0.38 to 0.78 ms, from 45 ms (e316f57). `orientation:` and a time of day in `date:` (bdcaab5, 04548fb). Text ignores accents and width everywhere (DEC-52): the small tables, `kw:`, `collection:` and the text index, built again as schema version 7 in 5.7 to 9.6 s at a million (c77863b, f46f004); free text under three characters still matched against the small tables (db5793d), its lookups in one pass (c9570aa); search p95 at a million 3.7 to 3.9 ms. A trait for the moments without a pick, `is:unpicked-moment`, evaluated in one pass over the source's photos in capture order and kept for each version of the store, source and setting (6ea2f63). `is:damaged` is found without Library Health's full check and kept across store changes, at p95 2.6 to 3.0 ms after a change (577b93c). The name order is Folders' own, one `FileOrder` key for both, in the column store, the SQL collation, facets, keyword lists, collections and groups (5e493f4); names with accents or other non-ASCII letters sort ignoring case, accents and width; the column snapshot is format 2, rebuilt once at the next launch; Lightroom keyword files are written in that order. Not done: Wide Open, Telephoto and Ultra Wide, which need the lens's widest aperture and the 35 mm-equivalent focal length in the index. Done when it reaches main | The owner (2026-10-05); [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations); [Katami §5](notes/LIB-katami.md#5-proposed-tracker-changes); [LIB-cling](notes/LIB-cling.md) |
| LIB-07 | Indexing at scale: one read per file for its capture metadata (EXIF, GPS, IPTC, XMP) and its thumbnail; resumable, visible and recent folders first; I/O scheduled per volume (many readers on SSDs, one or two in folder order on spinning disks, several in flight on network volumes); paused in Low Power Mode and when the Mac is hot | Build | P4 | L | LIB-05 | Accepted (2026-10-05): in the core the owner chose for 1.0 (DEC-46) | In progress: built on library/catalog (350cbb9 to 29eb29e): one read per file, per-volume readers, resumable; 20,000 photos in 17 s on the SSD. At a million (f568170 to 10ea381): a crash about a thousand batches in fixed (each commit nested a closure one level deeper); all 1,000,000 photos indexed in 2,153 s, 464 a second at a load average of 80, with no failures and a peak of 1.33 GB; the first 1,000 searchable in 3.5 s, from 30.7 s, once listings stopped going ahead of every photo read; the folders on screen really first (b05c321: other folders' photos had been read before the folder on screen was listed). Other apps' metadata is read with LibraryXMP's own code, field by field (935d217). The two races Thread Sanitizer flagged weren't races: the Swift runtime doesn't tell it about the ordering it gives a read that finished before its caller waited; results now also pass through a lock, and its runs are clean (ec81312). A change only the `.redlamp` has reads no photo again: a keyword on 10,000 photos is indexed in 15 to 23 s with none read again, where 3,369 were in 49 to 53 s (1463ba3); with schema version 4's fields, the 20,000-photo index build took 20.0 and 20.9 s, against 32.5 and 23.4 before. Keyword and metadata batches keep the index's date for sidecars they leave as they are, and the XMP sync records the dates of the sidecars it writes, so change tracking reads none of them again (805d3f3 to 78b9c8f). The folders asked for finish before other folders start (d440c1a), and another app's shifted capture time is read from its `.xmp` (1212a92). An indexing batch no longer merges the text index in its commit (2401e5a): at a million a batch is held p95 27 ms from 32, and the build from nothing to a million takes 23.0 s from 25.7. A batch's change to a photo stands over what change tracking read before it (740924c, 35575eb): metadata and keyword batches register their photos from their first index write until their sidecars' dates are recorded, and the indexer writes a read only if the photo isn't registered and its row is as its listing found it, otherwise reading it again in the same run once the batch is done, so another app's `.xmp` and another Mac's `.redlamp` still arrive; `IndexerStaleReadTests` failed before, losing a keyword, a collection, and a rating and label for good. Text merges take each transaction's result in the order they ran, so a merge step's late result can't end a later write's wait early (94302e7); a new test fails without it. Not done: the 2 s first-results budget, to check on a quiet Mac; a signal 11 seen once in a warm launch at 400,000 photos, not reproduced since. Done when it reaches main | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| LIB-08 | Change detection without rescanning: FSEvents replayed per volume from the last event at launch, folder signatures where that history is gone, network volumes polled with backoff; photos renamed or moved in Finder found by their sidecar's ID, then file identity, then size and a partial hash; a restored or replaced volume matched again by its folders and IDs; rescans that propose changes and never drop a record; and a volume that can't be reached never blocking anything | Build | P4 | L | LIB-07 | Accepted (2026-10-05): in the core the owner chose for 1.0 (DEC-46) | In progress: built on library/catalog (abfccc7): FSEvents replayed per volume, folder signatures when the history is gone, network volumes polled, Finder renames keep their rows. At a million photos (15f6b19 to e9e86ec): a warm launch visible and searchable in 0.42 s, from 12 s, with the index shown at once and reconciled behind it (17 to 37 s by folder signatures, 0.3 to 0.5 s from the event history); a volume that answers slowly no longer taken for gone, so the vanishing-volume scenario passes. `.caughtUp(volume:)` once each volume's first pass has run to its end, and again when it's back (8cc47c7); the change-tracking test flaky under load waits for its own FSEvents stream to see each change, 150 of 150 runs passing (3434706). The app's library service marks a volume's roots current on `.caughtUp` and no longer on `.volumeOffline` (41e7332). At launch the library takes out the roots Folders lost while it was off, import destinations kept, and change tracking stops following a removed root (24459d2). The folder watcher's streams start and stop on a queue of their own, off the main thread, the new one running before the old one stops (f93ab9e). A session replaced as Folders changes leaves nothing going: one still finding its volumes no longer follows them under the next, which brought a folder just removed back into the library, and its worker no longer takes the next session's work and drops it (88bc0f5); `ChangeTrackerSessionTests` failed 3 times in 3 before. Not done: a run on a real network share. Done when it reaches main | The owner (2026-10-05); [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations) |
| LIB-09 | Thumbnail and preview store for a million photos: keyed by photo identity so renames and moves keep them, a grid tier and a screen-size tier, kept for every indexed photo so slow, network and disconnected drives browse from the cache, with a size budget, a location setting and rebuilds (today's packs are keyed by path and capped at 1 GB) | Build | P4 | L | LIB-07 | Accepted (2026-10-05): in the core the owner chose for 1.0 (DEC-46) | In progress: built on library/catalog (9aad7ab to 9251b19, b615590, 84227c5): content-keyed shards with index files, budgets, eviction, compaction and moves; JPEG thumbnails at quality 0.5 (about 22 KB) and previews at 0.6 (about 560 KB), the owner's choice (2026-10-05); at a million records a random read in p99 4.7 µs and 34 bytes a record in memory. The app's Folders filmstrip takes its thumbnails from it (9dc4432). A write that finds an eviction running has it look at its tier again, so a tier no longer stays over its budget after concurrent writes (16af837). Not done: the grid on it (LIB-14), and the thumbnail maker reading through each volume's readers in a lane of its own. Done when it reaches main | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| LIB-10 | Photo lists: every source (a folder, a collection, a smart collection, a search, All Photographs) as an array of photo IDs and every selection as a bitset, behind the filmstrip and the grid, with folders keeping their behaviour and their `--folders-perf` budgets; a folder shows the photos in its subfolders by default, its count theirs too, as Lightroom Classic's Show Photos in Subfolders does, which turns it off | Build | P4 | L | LIB-06 | Accepted (2026-10-05): in the core the owner chose for 1.0 (DEC-46) | In progress: built on library/catalog (6d7674f to 92711c4): each source's photo IDs in a sort, with diffs; selections as a bit per photo ID; `LibraryLive` keeping open lists current from the indexer's events. At a million photos a list builds in 3 to 9 ms and a diff after 1,000 changes takes 7.2 ms. In the app, the Folders filmstrip lists indexed folders from them and unindexed ones from the disk, switching over without a jump, behind `LibraryEnabled` (9dc4432 to 9a11559). Collections and smart collections as sources (74009ec). Folders show their subfolders' photos and count them by default, as Lightroom Classic does (ce2b68e6): the setting, kept under a new key so every user starts with it on, turns it off; the Folders panel's counts come from one query on the index off the main thread, at most once a second, only changed rows drawn again (main thread p99 2.48 ms at lib-1m while counts change); folders the index doesn't have yet list from the disk. Every source a library view (d2c8397 to 2b18f69): the grid, the right panels, Group By, Tighter–Looser and the filter bar, with its narrowing counts, on All Photographs, Previous Import, Marked, Rejected, Library Health's checks, collections and smart collections, each source's list filtered and sorted off the main thread, and Previous Import a source of the photos it copied; on a 10,000-photo collection the panels follow the selection within 6.8 ms and Group By is on screen at p95 8.0 to 9.4 ms. A folder removed from Folders leaves the library (2265afb to 0d5ce4e): marked in the index, it's out of the store, searches, the palette, Library Health, the counts and every open list at once, its rows swept in batches after; a removal a quit cuts short finishes at the next launch; a missing root (an unplugged disk) keeps its photos; adding the folder back restores everything from its sidecars. On 150,000 photos of a copy of lib-1m: out of the store and searches in 83 ms, out of a shown Rejected in 123 ms, the counts in 242 ms. A shown Previous Import follows a newer import (641b593). Since (cdca918 to 900bf69): duplicates, custom-label counts, Write .xmp for All Photos, the import's skip list and the statistics leave a removed root's photos out before the sweep; the panels count again however a folder leaves, as the library opens too; the main thread's p99 1.5 to 2.0 ms during a removal; All Photographs takes the change rather than building its list again, 1.9 s at a million from 3.4. A swept root's photos take their health findings, hashes and XMP merge records with them (7bece12), and stacks' readers leave a removed root out (4dd15ea). All Photographs at a million opens in 4.4 s, from 14.1, its rows read in one pass over their IDs on three of the index's four readers (852a96d to e4f36a7). A sweep's batch holds other writes only as long as its own deletions, its text index merged after in steps and paced against the checkpoints (edbf7e3, 4c5c983): during a 150,000-photo sweep another write waits a median of 0.1 to 0.5 ms and p99 15 to 20 ms, from 86 and 1,479, and the sweep takes 7 s from 12. Sources keyed by the index's IDs, with no tables keyed by URL (3d3d0fc, f43e436): a photo found by its folder and name, the selection following a photo through filter changes, the photos the app lists itself given IDs 2,097,152 above the index's; All Photographs at a million opens in 3.2 s from 4.4 and takes a removal of 150,000 in 1.0 s from 1.9, main thread p99 1.8 to 2.3 ms. All Photographs at a million opens in 16 to 20 ms and takes a removal of 150,000 photos in 99 to 103 ms, against 300 (from 3.2 s and 1.0 s), the main thread's p99 1.9 to 2.0 ms during it: a source of more than 50,000 photos hands over its IDs with its first 1,000 rows and reads the rest as their cells appear (aa55d2b to 1e568b1), what acts on photos out of sight reads their rows first or takes their IDs (41bd459), and a folder shown from the library takes the index's IDs from its list (5e15877). A folder of more than 50,000 photos shown from the library reads its rows as its cells appear too, and sorts each folder's photos by name as Folders does (a186e95, 82cc61f): lib-1m's 2024 (50,066 photos) opens in 47 ms from 660, Clients (150,000) in 62 ms from 2.0 s, the whole library (a million) in 0.87 s from 15.3. Large folders take that order without reading names: 50,066 photos in 43 ms, 150,000 in 45 to 48, a million in 0.70 s (c966942). An action on the whole of a large selection starts at once (928a7ad): Sync, Paste, Auto Sync and Update AI Masks take the selection's IDs and read URLs off the main thread 256 at a time, Show in Finder and Remove Dust read them off the main thread, panel changes, keyword drops and stacking take IDs; the first URLs in 0.7 to 1.1 ms where every row took 2.35 s. Photos a batch moves are followed by their IDs until the folder's list has them where they're shown, so a folder never shows a photo twice or where it was (ab06a0a): under load the catalog showed 1,001 photos in a folder of 1,000. Not done: a large folder stays large while shown, its recently written photos don't wait to settle, and one taken over from a disk listing loses a multi-selection; names to sort as the Finder does (DEC-58), to build. Done when it reaches main | The owner (2026-10-05; subfolders by default, 2026-10-07) |

Move Edits and Metadata… in the app (a20c381 to b04c47c): from a root's menu in Folders, the Library menu and the palette; its sheet says where the folder's edits and metadata are kept, where they'd go and how many photos have them, and refuses a move into a folder Redlamp can't write in, with the reason; the move runs in journaled parts of 256 in the library's changes' turn, the open photo's edit saved first, Cancel putting back what moved and a quit finished at the next launch; moving 10,000 photos keeps the main thread's p99 at 1.8 to 7.0 ms against 8.3; scenario `library.move-edits`. The library's move is cancellable, one journal for the whole move turned round on Cancel and finished at the next launch, with a public write check that says why not (410cc6d); the count reads in about 1 ms at 150,000 photos through schema 8's index (076d19b). The turns holding the menu bar's rebuild during Move Edits from 213 to 311 ms to 3 to 5 ms, the longest main-thread stall during its moves from 308 and 331 ms to 49 and 18 ms (f26e595). The sheet opens at once, where the locator says the root keeps the edits, and doesn't wait on the index's read (ef0883e): the read is over 0.5 to 1 ms after the command, the sheet on screen 23 to 35 ms after it with the numbers in it. The 46 ms was the regression suite choosing the item while the menu was opening; a click runs it once the menu has closed, as the scenarios now do (8ca67b4). Its sheet keeps its window, on screen in 23 to 25 ms from its second showing (8e241e8, 26746a5). | LIB-14 | Grid and loupe: an AppKit grid with sizes and cell styles; keyboard navigation and selection; mouse selection (click, ⌘-click, ⇧-click, rubber band, double-click to open); menus, context menus and the toolbar for every action; each source's view remembered | Adopt | P4 | L | LIB-09, LIB-13 | Accepted (2026-10-05): in the core the owner chose for 1.0 (DEC-46) | In progress: built on library/catalog (with LIB-13, then 92c39fa to 5d9a238): cells as layers recycled by row, thumbnails drawn off the main thread, scrolling in main-thread p99 1.25 ms against 8.3; sizes from 80 to 400 points by = and - or a slider; J's three cell styles; rubber-band, click, ⌘-click and ⇧-click selection on photo IDs shared with the filmstrip; context menus and a Library toolbar; each source's size, style, place and selection kept; the Loupe's Fit and 1:1 with Z. The grid's budgets (086a639a to fd305af4): the main-thread stall of 7 to 12 s in every `--library-perf` run was App Nap throttling the hidden app and the harness's own work on the main thread, both gone; turns over half a second are sampled in every phase; the filmstrip lays out one row, placing only the cells on screen, so a reload or a filter's change costs a screenful at any count, and follows the selection by its clip view, since main's strip without scrollers (UX-19) no longer scrolls to items (60cb2bf6); an expanded cell's details are found whichever of Unicode's forms a name is in (5ae09c80). Held arrow keys at 20,000 at main-thread p99 5.8 to 7.9 ms ungrouped (from 9.5), 9.1 to 9.3 ms through groups against 8.3 (22146909 to 0408a4ea). A source of more than 50,000 photos gives the grid and filmstrip its rows as their cells appear (aa55d2b to 1e568b1), lib-20k's numbers as before: the filmstrip's 20,000 photos in 241 ms, scrolling at p99 2.7 ms, expanded cells 2.6 ms, held arrows 2.5 to 2.6 ms. The menu bar reads `MenuBarState`, so SwiftUI rebuilds it (2.5 to 3 ms) only when what an item shows changes: held arrows at 20,000 from 400 rebuilds in 200 presses to 2, the checks 0.34 ms a change; `performance.menu-bar` fails at one rebuild in ten presses (f26e595 to ff77f7a). A ⌘ key's item is brought up to date before AppKit looks for it (`MenuBarKeys`): AppKit's search doesn't open the menu, and SwiftUI sets a top-level menu's targets only as it opens, so ⌘R did nothing for a photo selected since the Photo menu last showed Show in Finder disabled; scenario `menus.keys-after-menu` (561b1f0). An item first asked about after the checks narrow shows the model's answer at once (dd1ec2d). `AppCommands`' menus are built in parts, the slowest type-checking in 50 ms against 309 to 347 (fbb771f). Held →: the filmstrip moves only when the photo leaves its middle half and centres once the key pauses, and the Keywording, Metadata and Keyword List panels set only what changes (87c0832), the p99 at 20,000 from 11.43 ms to 9.07. Not done: held → at 20,000 at a main-thread p99 of 9.07 ms against 8.3, the right panel and the filmstrip about 2.3 ms each a press (`performance.held-arrows-parts`); accepted at that while a key is held, the panels following every step (DEC-57). Rename Photos and Move to Folder in the app (3c798ab0 to a11ef129): a live preview naming every photo off the main thread, empty tokens flagged and collisions numbered in capture order; one journaled batch with progress, photos keeping their IDs so the selection follows; Undo and Redo on ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z in turn with culling's; Move to Folder from an Open panel that refuses folders outside the library; Put Back undone and redone on ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z through the journal (19f2a864). Photos dragged onto a folder move there as one batch with Undo, its progress in the grid's toolbar, and a drop on their own folder or one outside the library is refused (ef74121, 99222d2); a 1,000-photo drop at main-thread p99 4.9 to 5.7 ms. ⌘Z is there in the turn a drop's move ends (ef47bbb), and renames, moves and Put Backs take their place on Undo when they're asked for. Library Health's batches go to the Trash through the file journal, with Undo and Put Back, and Recently Trashed follows big batches without `lstat` on the main thread (e5e6c9c). ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z while a drop's move runs wait for it, then take it back and make it again (1afc31c). A batch holds the folders its steps change, once listings of them already under way are done, until its index is written, and the indexer lists or probes a held folder only after that; the index's writes delete a vanished row only from the folder it vanished from and drop a late write for a row a batch has moved since (20429d5, 325986d). Moved photos keep their IDs, edits, keywords and collections through a move, its Undo and its Redo, whatever change tracking reads meanwhile: `FileBatchListingTests` failed before, A and C getting new IDs and Redo moving neither. Copying and Stop (1c8d905 to d7e8b60): ⌥ on a drop and Copy to Folder… copy photos as a journaled batch finished or rolled back at launch, each copy with a new ID, its original's sidecars and none of its collections, a taken name numbered as the Finder does, and Undo moving the copies to the Trash; renames, moves and copies, and their Undo and Redo, stop after the photo in hand, leaving one Undo step for what was done. Copies are clones on their own volume and on another read once as they're copied, then read back: 1,000 raws (32.5 GB) within the SSD in 5.3 s from 570.8, to the internal disk in 123.1 s from 356.9 (eb0bf35); moves across volumes read the original once; Folders takes a batch's listings together each turn with counts in place, and a rename's or move's URLs are found off the main thread (6d4ff78, 7f3b04f), renaming, moving and the rename's Undo at main-thread p99 1.75 to 1.92 ms; `redlamp library copy` (823f136); a stopped rename's Undo takes out the original names only of the photos it put back (a0a2121). Copy to Folder… is in the Photo menu (6adca46). Move and Copy to Folder show their progress and Stop in the grid's toolbar, as a drop, an Undo and a Redo do (f1906bd): a move of 1,000 photos in 2.5 s with no main-thread turn over 34 ms, where its sheet cost two turns of about 400 ms; Rename Photos writes its presets file off the main thread (8e241e8); a folder never shows a photo twice or where it was while a batch moves it (ab06a0a). Not done: speed against the 1 s budget, which this Mac's disk can't meet; every sheet (Rename Photos, Move Edits) holding the main thread about 0.3 s as it opens and as it closes, in AppKit's run loop, whether they stay sheets the owner's call; the move's Undo of 1,000 photos at p99 5.8 to 8.6 ms, 3 or 4 turns of 42 to 58 ms in the grid, the filmstrip and SwiftUI. Done when it reaches main | The owner (2026-10-05); [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations); [Katami §5](notes/LIB-katami.md#5-proposed-tracker-changes) |

The store is saved beside the index as `Index.columns`, one page-aligned file: a 16 KB header (format, page size, schema version, a generation with a random token, each section's checksum), then each column, the sort orders and the small tables' names on 16 KB boundaries, 85 MB at a million. It's written atomically after a build, once the store and index have been quiet for 10 s (at most five minutes apart) and when asked, only the header when just the generation moved. At launch it's mapped copy-on-write when its generation and schema match, read ahead with one request when cold; one from another generation, schema, format or page size is deleted, a truncated or damaged one set aside, and the store built from SQLite. At a million photos a relaunch is searchable in 106 and 224 ms, from 505 and 509, and memory over launch and idle after a trim is 28 to 32 MB, from 225 to 236. The app saves it as it quits and trims the engine under memory pressure (ce965dc). The store leaves a marked root's photos out, in a snapshot saved mid-removal too (2265afb). Not done: a run with the file cache emptied. Done when it reaches main | [LIB-cling §4](notes/LIB-cling.md#4-the-column-store-mapped-from-a-file-lib-05-lib-06) |

## 14. Recorded skips

Decisions not to do something, kept so they aren't reopened without new evidence.

| ID | Skip | Why | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SKIP-01 | User-reorderable processing modules | darktable's own manual recommends against changing the order | [DT §5](darktable-findings.md#5-modules-filters-and-masks) |
| SKIP-02 | Generic module instances and blend modes | Mask layers are Redlamp's instances | [DT §5](darktable-findings.md#5-modules-filters-and-masks) |
| SKIP-03 | Multiple tone mappers, a second white-balance step or a second denoiser as separate panels | darktable's most confusing designs | [DT §4](darktable-findings.md#4-color-science-and-tone) |
| SKIP-04 | Database-first edit storage | Causes darktable's sync crawler and conflict dialogs | [DT §6](darktable-findings.md#6-edits-presets-styles-and-versioning) |
| SKIP-05 | Binary parameter blobs in sidecars | Unreadable and unmergeable | [DT §6](darktable-findings.md#6-edits-presets-styles-and-versioning) |
| SKIP-06 | Chorded shortcuts, exposed performance and GPU tuning, separate CPU code paths | Complexity without user value on unified-memory Apple Silicon | [DT §8](darktable-findings.md#8-architecture-performance-and-ux) |
| SKIP-07 | Liquify, projection changes, lens "simulate" mode | Not in Lightroom; low value | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| SKIP-08 | rawspeed, the lensfun library, darktable data files, AMaZE and RCD | Licenses don't fit an MPL-2.0 App Store app | [DT §9](darktable-findings.md#9-licensing-of-reusable-pieces) |
| SKIP-09 | PatchMatch-based healing | Adobe patents active to about 2031 | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |
| SKIP-10 | Diffusion upscalers and generative fill | Hallucination risk; non-commercial or restrictive licenses | [AI §4](ai-findings.md#4-b-super-resolution-and-upscaling) |
| SKIP-11 | Sky replacement and relighting | Not in Lightroom; conflicts with truthful editing | [AI §8](ai-findings.md#8-f-other-opportunities) |
| SKIP-12 | Studying vkdt for now | Owner decision, 30 September 2026 | Conversation |
| SKIP-13 | Face-specific generative restoration (GFPGAN, CodeFormer, RestoreFormer, face diffusion) | Changes identity details (in the bake-off, a dark eye turned blue and stubble appeared); every model is trained on FFHQ (non-commercial) | [H §6.4](notes/H-topaz-upscale-sharpen.md#64-faces) |
| SKIP-14 | libgphoto2 for tethering (darktable's camera library) | LGPL-2.1, and its maker tables are reverse-engineered data, both excluded by the clean-room policy | [TC routes](notes/TC-routes-and-licences.md) |
| SKIP-15 | A cloud review service like Capture One Live | Redlamp has no cloud; clients see captures on a second display or an iPad on the local network (TET-16) | [TC teardown](notes/TC-capture-one-teardown.md#review-and-clients) |
| SKIP-16 | Makers' camera SDKs for tethering (Sony Camera Remote SDK, Canon EDSDK, Nikon SDK, Fujifilm Camera Control SDK) | The owner's decision (2026-10-05, DEC-29): nothing from makers' SDKs; Redlamp's own code on Apple's frameworks and open protocols. The SDKs can't be in an MPL-2.0 repository, compete with macOS's `ptpcamerad` over USB, and Sony's and Fujifilm's require a warranty notice to users | [TC routes](notes/TC-routes-and-licences.md#4-licence-matrix) |
| SKIP-17 | Re-measuring noise reduction's Bayer noise table through the Menon demosaic (it was measured through Malvar's, which Menon replaced the same day) | Measured on 7 October: at equal texture kept it scores within 0.1 dB of the current table on the DN-11 scenes and the RawNIND Bayer pairs; it only makes each Luminance setting weaker, and the sliders were tuned on the current table. The calibration check now measures Bayer through Malvar | [DN-11 §9](notes/DN-11-lightroom-raw-denoise.md#9-follow-up-the-bayer-noise-table-skip-17-7-october) |
| SKIP-18 | A catalog file as the library's source of truth, with an import step that hides the folders (Lightroom Classic's model) | The owner's decision (2026-10-05, DEC-42): folders on disk organise photos and each photo's metadata stays in its sidecar, so nothing is locked in a catalog | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| SKIP-19 | A shared multi-user database, or real-time multi-user sessions | The owner's decision (2026-10-05): the index is per Mac and can always be rebuilt (DEC-42), and sidecars let a team share the files | [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations) |
| SKIP-20 | Several catalogs, and merging them | The owner's decision (2026-10-05): a library of folders rebuilds its index and has nothing to merge (DEC-42) | [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations) |
| SKIP-21 | Live two-way sync with other apps' catalogs | The owner's decision (2026-10-05): their private formats would need an update for each of their releases; Redlamp reads a copy instead (LIB-29, LIB-30) | [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations) |
